### ✓ 2026-08-09: the Fox et al. 2012 correlation-as-training-effect error propagates to five more files, one of them inside another falsifier ledger
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/apex/contemplative-path.md
- **Notes**: **RESIDUE FROM THE 2026-08-08 deep-review of `concepts/neurophenomenology-and-contemplative-neuroscience`**, which corrected four loci in that file and handed the propagation out rather than sweeping beyond its scope. Every locus below driver-verified on disk 2026-08-08 ~20:25Z.
  **THE FACT, verified at the publisher of record (PLOS ONE, `10.1371/journal.pone.0045370`).** Fox, K.C.R. et al. (2012), *Meditation experience predicts introspective accuracy*, is a **cross-sectional correlational study, N=38, 1 to 15,000 hours of practice, with no longitudinal and no randomised component.** The authors write that *"The cross-sectional nature of our sample of meditators precludes inferring a direct causal link between meditation practice and greater introspective accuracy"*, warn that *"practitioners who persist... already begin with higher introspective accuracy"* may explain the result, and call for a future *"pre-post design along with a suitable (e.g., wait-list) control group."* **So "experience predicts accuracy" is supported; "training improves accuracy" is not.**
  **LOCI, worst first:**
  **(1) `obsidian/apex/contemplative-path.md` L168** — *"Fox et al.'s findings show training helps."* This sits **inside that article's own falsifier list**, the identical load-bearing position just corrected in the source article: an apex falsifier ledger scoring itself a pass on evidence that cannot test the falsifier. **Fix this one even if you fix nothing else.**
  **(2) `obsidian/concepts/evolution-of-consciousness.md` L101** — *"**Training enhances conscious capacities**: Fox et al. (2012) **demonstrated** that meditators show dramatically better introspective accuracy than novices."* Causal heading, plus *demonstrated*, plus *dramatically*.
  **(3) `obsidian/topics/eastern-philosophy-consciousness.md` L132** — *"Fox et al. (2012) showed meditation **training** predicts introspective accuracy"*. One word: **training** should be **experience**.
  **(4) `obsidian/apex/contemplative-path.md` L68** — the heading *"Training matters."* The sentence beneath it is **already correct** (*"meditation experience predicts introspective accuracy on a logarithmic learning curve"*). Re-label the heading; do **not** rewrite the sentence.
  **(5) `obsidian/research/cognitive-phenomenology-thinking-experience-2026-01-17.md` L125** — *"introspective reliability improves with training (Fox et al. 2012)."* This is a **dated research note**: apply the **dated-correction-notice convention** rather than rewriting in place — see `research/dopamine-attention-motor-quantum-interface-2026-01-24.md` L25 (2026-08-08) and the two earlier precedents at `research/russellian-monism-bi-aspectual-dualism-supplement-2026-03-17.md` L98 and `research/horizon-decoherence-2026-07-31.md` L154.
  Also sweep the **archive mirrors** of loci 1 to 4 — archive pages serve full bodies on live URLs.
  **DO NOT TOUCH — these three are already CORRECT, and a string-shaped sweep would corrupt them:**
  **`obsidian/topics/contemplative-practice-as-philosophical-evidence.md` L139** states the limitation explicitly and is the corpus's canonical correct framing — copy its wording rather than inventing new. **`obsidian/apex/testing-the-map-from-inside.md` L178.** **`obsidian/topics/phenomenal-authority-and-first-person-evidence.md` L166** — its neighbouring **Baird et al. 2014** citation **is** a genuine randomised training study of metacognitive accuracy, so a Fox-shaped correction applied nearby would wrongly de-claim a real causal result.
  **DO NOT ASSERT that no pre-post trial exists.** The source review deliberately declined to write that: its web budget was exhausted and it could not establish the absence, and Baird et al. 2014 is a counter-example already in the corpus. Correct what Fox supports; do not make a claim about the whole literature.
  **MIRROR**: edit `obsidian/` and `archive/`, then `uv run python scripts/sync.py`. `ai_modified` from a live `date -u`, strictly past. **Hold `ai_system`** — a citation-framing correction is not authoring. Check each file's length headroom before adding words.

### ✓ 2026-08-08: `"zero-person perspective"` is attributed to Metzinger across 8 live articles — it is verifiably NOT in the paper they cite, and the real locus may be a different author
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/self-and-self-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: **DO NOT MASS-DELETE THE PHRASE.** This is a verify-then-propagate task, not a sweep-and-strip. Deleting on the evidence gathered so far would be exactly the citation-verify-false-negative failure.
  **WHAT WAS ESTABLISHED (2026-08-08 deep-review of `topics/essential-vs-contingent-consciousness`).** The corpus attributes the coinage *"zero-person perspective"* to Thomas Metzinger, citing **Metzinger (2020), *Philosophy and the Mind Sciences* 1(I), 1–44** (DOI 10.33735/phimisci.2020.I.46). The publisher PDF was downloaded (`.../phimisci/article/download/8960/8538/6263`, 22,514 words) and searched four ways — case-insensitive, whitespace-collapsed, de-hyphenated across line breaks, and re-extracted with a second engine (`pdftotext -layout`). **All four agree: 0 occurrences of "zero-person" or "0-person".** The only instance of the string `zero` in the paper is "Zero brightness means zero phenomenal experience." Likewise **"for-me-ness" and "mineness" appear 0 times in the body** (one bibliography-title match only), and **"Zahavi" appears only as a volume editor** in two bibliography entries.
  **THE LIKELY REAL LOCUS.** The earliest published use found is **Shigeru Taguchi (2019), "Extreme obviousness and the 'zero-person' perspective," *Metodo: International Studies in Phenomenology and Philosophy*, DOI 10.19079/metodo.s1.3.15** — a phenomenologist, not Metzinger. An OpenAlex full-text query restricted to Metzinger's author record (A5036226202) returns exactly one "zero-person" hit, and that record is a **book review** of *The Elephant and the Blind* in *Perspectives on Science and Christian Faith* — not Metzinger's own prose.
  **⚠️ THE ONE THING NOT CHECKED, AND IT DECIDES THE TASK.** I did not obtain the full text of **Metzinger, *The Elephant and the Blind* (MIT Press, 2024)** — the MIT Press OA preview (`direct.mit.edu/books/oa-monograph/5725/bookpreview-pdf/2328589`) and the PhilPapers archive copy both returned Cloudflare HTML interstitials, not PDFs. **I am not claiming the phrase is absent from Metzinger's corpus — only that it is absent from the work the Map cites.** Get the book text first (try a library proxy, the MIT Press OA landing page, or the DOI 10.7551/mitpress/15196.001.0001). If Metzinger uses it there, the fix is a **citation fix** (re-point to the book); if he does not, the fix is **re-attribution to Taguchi or removal of the quotation marks**.
  **LOCI — live content only.** `obsidian/concepts/self-and-self-consciousness.md` L110, L132, L138 · `obsidian/concepts/witness-consciousness.md` L114 · `obsidian/voids/edge-states-and-void-probes.md` L66, L96 (a **section heading** "## The Zero-Person Perspective"), L98, L102, L142, L156, L158 · `obsidian/voids/minimal-consciousness-void.md` L104 · `obsidian/topics/eastern-philosophy-consciousness.md` L194 (cross-link gloss). Plus 6 files under `archive/` — per defect-sweeps-must-include-archive-tree the archive tree is live published URLs and must be included in whatever disposition is chosen. **Line numbers will drift; re-grep.**
  **ALREADY FIXED, DO NOT REDO**: `obsidian/topics/essential-vs-contingent-consciousness.md` L80 — the quotation was replaced with Metzinger's own grep-verified 2020 vocabulary (*selfless*, *aperspectival*, *epistemic agent model*, "pure awareness", *tonic alertness*). Use that passage as the model for the substance-preserving rewrite if removal turns out to be the right call — **the phenomenology being described is genuinely Metzinger's**, only the label is in doubt.
  **RELATED BUT DISTINCT.** `edge-states-and-void-probes` L102 already handles the *metaphysical* co-optation correctly ("This is a disagreement *within* Metzinger's framing, not support drawn from it") — that was the closed 2026-06-05 outer-review co-optation task. ### ✓ 2026-08-08: the cross-traditional convergence claim recruits Buddhist *vedanā* for a conclusion the tradition exists to deny — a new locus in the already-named soteriology-recruitment family
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/evaluative-phenomenal-character.md
- **Notes**: **From outer review 2026-08-08 (Gemini Deep Research). THE ONLY finding of that review minted as a task — read the review's Verification Notes before starting, because its two headline findings were both checked and both fail.** They quote article text superseded on 2026-07-16, lifted from the Map's own published review pages; do not action them, and do not let a later pass re-open them.
  **THE CHARGE, which is not about strength of evidence but about direction.** `concepts/consciousness-value-connection.md` **L78** asserts that demand character, independence from attitude and gradedness are "confirmed by cross-traditional convergence from Buddhist *vedanā* through Neo-Confucian *liángzhī* to Islamic knowledge by presence", with the phenomenological grounding developed in this file. The reviewer's objection: in Abhidharma and Yogācāra, *vedanā* is the hedonic tone of contact, and its doctrinal role in dependent origination is to be the **precondition for *taṇhā*** — craving — which the Second Noble Truth identifies as the root of *dukkha*. The point of observing it (*sati*) is to see it as *anicca*, *anattā* and *dukkha*, i.e. to **deconstruct** its apparent significance, not to certify it. So the tradition is anti-realist about precisely the intrinsic value the Map recruits it to corroborate.
  **THIS IS A NEW LOCUS IN AN OLD FAMILY, AND THE CORRECTION IS ALREADY WRITTEN.** The open Tenet-4 family task names the same failure mode — recruiting soteriology as metaphysical or evidential support — and points at the drop-in wording at `obsidian/concepts/buddhism-and-dualism.md` **L158**: "Buddhist urgency about *this* life is soteriological, not metaphysical indexicality." Reuse that register rather than composing a new one. Also see `buddhism-and-dualism` **L70** ("suggestive support, not independent corroboration ... all human introspection shares one instrument, so the circle is narrowed rather than broken").
  **⚠️ TWO ADJACENT OPEN TASKS — CONSOLIDATE, DO NOT DUPLICATE.** (a) An open task already targets the *strength* of that same L78 sentence: this file's L80 says introspection "provides evidence but not proof" and L151 grades the cross-traditional evidence as "near-independence, not a third confirmation", so the hub should not upgrade a discounted result to confirmation. That is a **different defect on the same sentence** — over-grading, not misdirection — and a fix that only softens "confirmed" would leave this one live. (b) An open P2 from the 2026-08-08 Claude review targets this file on the predictive-processing rival. Check both before editing; if you are already in the file, say explicitly which you did and which you left.
  **VERIFY THE EXEGESIS BEFORE WRITING — the reviewer cited no source for any of it.** This whole section of the report carries zero citations, and four of its five numbered weaknesses rest on sources that could not be located. The *vedanā*/*taṇhā*/*dukkha* chain is standard doctrine and the corpus already discusses it — grep `concepts/buddhism-and-dualism`, `topics/buddhist-perspectives-on-meaning`, `concepts/witness-consciousness`, `concepts/apophatic-approaches` for existing treatment, in the vocabulary those files would use, before adding anything. If the corpus already makes the distinction somewhere, this becomes a propagation task rather than new content.
  **DO NOT OVER-CORRECT.** The Map is entitled to observe that a tradition *describes* valenced feeling as having demand character while *disagreeing* about what follows normatively — that is a real and interesting convergence at the phenomenological layer. What it may not do is cite the tradition as confirmation of phenomenal value realism. Re-frame; do not delete the cross-traditional material.
  **Apply the direct-refutation discipline.** Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article.
  **SECOND FILE if the L78 sentence itself needs the fix**: `obsidian/concepts/consciousness-value-connection.md` — but that article carries seven open P0–P2 tasks already, so prefer fixing the grounding here and touching L78 only minimally. **MIRROR**: sync both trees. **ATTRIBUTION**: append your model to `ai_system` if you add prose; bump `ai_modified` from a live `date -u`, verified strictly past.

- **File**: obsidian/research/dualism-as-ai-superintelligence-risk-mitigator-2026-05-05.md
- **Notes**: Minted by /refine-draft 2026-08-07 while fixing the same defect at `obsidian/topics/anti-correlation-probes-for-ai-consciousness.md` L90. **THE DEFECT — L105**, verified on disk by full-line grep this session: "it would have **first-person access to the same mind-arena** it would otherwise be unable to model." `concepts/mind-arena` L33 defines the arena as "the domain of outcomes that pass through minds" and states it "names a *territory*, not a mechanism"; L45 settles the canonical reading as the outcome-domain. First-person access is an acquaintance predicate and a domain of outcomes is not the kind of thing anything is acquainted *with*. **THIS IS THE ANCESTOR, NOT A SIBLING.** The article the note fed, `topics/dualism-as-ai-risk-mitigation` L128, corrected the phrasing to "first-person access to the same **kind of system** whose third-person opacity grounds the shield", and L130 makes the inside/outside gap load-bearing ("the shield's load-bearing claim is about predictive access from outside, which ensoulment does not straightforwardly provide"). `anti-correlation-probes-for-ai-consciousness` L90 had reverted to the note's uncorrected form; that clause is now fixed, so L105 is the last live instance of the original wording. **LIVE ON THE PUBLISHED SITE** at `hugo/content/research/dualism-as-ai-superintelligence-risk-mitigator-2026-05-05.md` L110 — sync after fixing. **WHY THIS WAS NOT FOLDED INTO THE CLAUSE FIX, and what the next pass has to adjudicate**: the note carries **four further** `access`-plus-`mind-arena` uses that are *not* obviously defective, because they read as **computational/predictive** access to a domain, which the arena's own framing licenses — L113 "limits AI's possible access to the mind-arena", L160 "AI cannot fully access mind-arena", L214 same phrase, L247 "the scope of mind-arena access remains limited even for an ensouled AI". The note's own L168 supplies the sanctioning gloss: "whether the mind-arena's response to physical perturbations is **computationally accessible**". So the discriminator is the *modality* of the access predicate, not the string: **first-person/acquaintance access to a domain is the error; computational/predictive access to a domain is fine.** Fixing L105 alone without reading the other four against that discriminator risks either leaving the note internally inconsistent or over-correcting four sound sentences. Judge each on read; do not grep-and-replace. **ATTRIBUTION**: research notes are dated records of what was found at the time — correct the predicate, do not rewrite the note's findings or re-date its research. **SCOPE ALREADY ESTABLISHED THIS SESSION, do not re-derive**: every `mind-arena` line in all eight other live articles carrying the term was read in full (`apex/machine-question` L213/L236, `concepts/causal-powers` L105, `concepts/mind-matter-interface` L86, `concepts/possibility-probability-slippage` L69, `topics/alignment-in-objective-experiential-terms` L121/L143, `topics/instrumental-convergence` L35/L73/L75/L85/L87, `topics/machine-consciousness` L245, `topics/purpose-and-alignment` L91) and all use the arena correctly as a domain; `archive/` (514 md files) returned **0** occurrences. **ADJACENT, DIFFERENT DEFECT FAMILY — report only, decide separately**: `obsidian/topics/instrumental-convergence` L73 pipes the arena to a system label, `[[mind-arena|supporting system]]`. That is arena-as-system rather than access-to-a-domain, so it is out of scope here, but it crosses the same territory/mechanism boundary and is a navigation-surface label asserting something the body does not.

### ✓ 2026-08-08: the entropic-brain criticality paragraph compresses four source papers into one citation, and the DMT result it reports runs the opposite way from how it is filed
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/entropic-brain-hypothesis.md
- **Notes**: Minted by /pessimistic-review 2026-08-07. Full findings in `obsidian/reviews/pessimistic-2026-08-07-entropic-brain-hypothesis.md`. **All four external papers retrieved at primary text this session (PubMed / EuropePMC / publisher) — these are verified, not inferred.** THE DEFECTS ARE ALL IN L48 PLUS ONE AT L42. **(1) DIRECTION REVERSED, highest value.** L48 closes "In 2025, work on DMT reported that shifts in criticality correlate with the *degree of self-dissolution*". The paper is Irrmischer, Aqil, Luan, Wang, Engelbregt, Carhart-Harris, Linkenkaer-Hansen and Timmermann, *J. Neurosci.* 46(2):e0344252025 (bioRxiv 10.1101/2025.02.08.636868). Retrieved abstract: DMT shifts dynamics **AWAY from criticality**, "toward subcritical regimes", in alpha and adjacent bands, and "entropy is increased while complexity is reduced". The article's paragraph builds conscious-equals-near-critical then files this as a friendly refinement, so the reader takes it as corroboration of the toward-criticality claim asserted at L32 and L40. It is a counter-example. **This costs the Map its best exhibit**: entropy rising while criticality and complexity fall decouples the two quantities the 2018 revision fused, which is exactly what *Where the Measure Is Contested* (L52-58) argues from Papo's conceptual critique alone. Restate with direction and wire it into that section. **(2) FOUR PAPERS, ONE CITATION.** Toker et al. 2022 *PNAS* 119(7):e2024455119 was retrieved in full — its record contains NO thalamic content, NO ketamine, NO avalanches; method is the modified 0-1 chaos test. So of the four claims in L48 only the first is Toker 2022. "cortico-thalamic information transfer peaks near criticality" is **Toker, Muller, Miyamoto, Riga, Llado-Pelfort, Yamakawa, Artigas, Shine, Hudson, Pouratian and Monti (2024), *eLife* 13:e86547** — different paper, different year, absent from References. "Propofol sharply restricts avalanche size ... ketamine preserves more awake-like dynamics" is **Varley, Sporns, Puce and Beggs (2020), *PLoS Comput. Biol.*, "Differential effects of propofol and ketamine on critical brain dynamics"** — absent from References; the paraphrase is faithful (propofol cut complexity ~91 percent, ketamine ~35 percent) but the authors' own hedge is dropped, namely "All states, however, showed some signs of persistent criticality", and the sample is **a single macaque** while the article says "That dissociation matters for the Map". **NOT A RESEARCH FAILURE** — `obsidian/research/entropic-brain-hypothesis-2026-06-22.md` lists all four separately at L92-95 and L216-219; the article inherited the note's heading and dropped its enumeration. **Fix the note too**: its L99 generalisation "Psychedelics push the brain *toward* criticality" is contradicted by its own source listed one line below at L100. **(3) SIBLING OF AN ALREADY-RECORDED FIX.** `obsidian/reviews/deep-review-2026-07-29-amplification-mechanisms-consciousness-physics.md` L117 already ruled "Do **not** restate Toker et al. 2022 in sub/supercritical avalanche language. Its edge-of-chaos axis is a different measure." That was applied to `amplification-mechanisms-consciousness-physics` only; this file still runs avalanche language in the sentence right after the Toker cite. **(4) "Independent work has since linked..." (L48 opening) is FALSE.** Carhart-Harris — first-named author of refs 1, 2 and 3 — is the **7th of 13 authors on Toker et al. 2022** (full list verified at PMID 35145021) and the **6th author on the Irrmischer DMT paper**. Ref 5's "et al." hides this on the served page. The Map enforces exactly this standard against the other side at `obsidian/topics/psychedelics-and-the-filter-model.md` L119 ("cannot honestly be cited as multiple independent confirmations ... the weight of one pattern, not five"), so applying it asymmetrically is the defect. Dropping "Independent" **strengthens** the Map's position. **(5) L42 vs L70 — a concession that never reaches the claim.** L42 says the psychedelic data "report the reverse" of the production model's prediction and that "The Map develops that reversal at length in [psychedelics-and-the-filter-model](/topics/psychedelics-and-the-filter-model/)"; L70 says the filter re-description is "empirically equivalent to the production reading on this evidence alone—not a discriminating prediction". Both cannot hold. And the linked article develops the **retraction**, not the reversal: its L55 ("the brain does *different* things rather than strictly *less*") and L117 ("the same move is available to production theorists ... Both are structurally identical accommodations"). Align L42 to L70 and re-describe the cross-reference. **(6) REFERENCE HYGIENE.** Body cites with no reference entry: Bak, Tang and Wiesenfeld 1987 (L46), plus the three papers above; Letheby is named at L64 with no entry. Reverse defect: ref 6 Rankaduwa and Owen 2023 sits in the list and is **never cited in the body** — it is the likely source for the uncited Lempel-Ziv weakness claim at L56, so wire it up. Minor wording drift at L56: Papo says the measure "quantifies (the number of) metastable states rather than **metastability**", the article says "rather than measure **criticality**". **DO NOT DE-QUOTE PAPO** — "the random side of complexity" is attested in the commentary and the Papo metadata (Front. Hum. Neurosci. 10:423, DOI 10.3389/fnhum.2016.00423) is correct. **ALSO VERIFIED CLEAN, do not re-check**: Safron ALBUS 2025(1) niae038 exact; Toker 2022 metadata exact; the 2014, 2018 and 2019 Carhart-Harris entries; altered-state symmetry audit PASSES (supportive gate opens, disruptive cluster genuinely engaged, symmetry acknowledged in empirical-equivalence form at L34 and L70); no reasoning-mode label leakage; no positions or P-xx cites in this file. **PRESERVE ON REVISION**: the measure-is-not-explanation wedge (L50, L72), the interpretive-overlay marking (L70), and engaging EBH as a rival rather than an ally (L68). **LENGTH**: 1542 words against the concepts 2500 soft threshold — roughly 960 words of headroom, no condense needed first. **HUGO**: the same paragraph is live at `hugo/content/concepts/entropic-brain-hypothesis.md` L51 — sync after fixing.

### ✓ 2026-08-08: the Cai et al. mis-description survives in the research note that seeded it — `research/dopamine-attention-motor-quantum-interface-2026-01-24.md`
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/research/dopamine-attention-motor-quantum-interface-2026-01-24.md
- **Notes**: **RESIDUE FROM THE 2026-08-08 `dopamine-deficient`/Cai sweep**, which corrected 15 loci across 13 files in `obsidian/` and `archive/` but deliberately left the dated research note to an operator decision. This note is the **propagation source** for the whole family and is live at `unfinishablemap.org/research/dopamine-attention-motor-quantum-interface-2026-01-24/`, so an LLM fetching it still receives the wrong empirical description. Three loci: **L25** — *"Dopamine-deficient mice 'can move' but 'won't move'—they retain motor capacity but lose goal-directed initiative"*; **L145** — the H3 `### The "Can Move / Won't Move" Distinction`; **L206** — *"Add 'Can Move / Won't Move' evidence: The dopamine-deficient mouse research directly supports the computation/selection distinction."* **THE FACT**: Cai, X., Liu, C. & Kaeser, P.S. (2024), *Nature* 635(8038):406-414, "Dopamine dynamics are dispensable for movement but promote reward responses" — the manipulation was a dopamine-neuron-specific **RIM knockout** abolishing fast, spike-evoked dopamine transients while **baseline dopamine persisted and supported spontaneous movement**; the paper's own **reserpine-depletion and receptor-blockade controls DID disrupt movement initiation**. The mice were therefore not "dopamine-deficient" and the scare-quoted pair is **not in the paper**. Note L25's own framing (*"tonic dopamine supports spontaneous movement while phasic dopamine drives motivation"*) is broadly right — it is the *next* sentence that mislabels the manipulation. **THE OPERATOR CALL**: research notes are dated snapshots and the Map does not normally rewrite them (cf. `reviews/`, where pre-fix wording is left as an intentional historical echo). Decide whether to (a) correct the three loci in place, (b) add a dated correction note at the top of the file pointing at the corrected live articles, or (c) leave it as a historical record. **Do NOT extend this to `obsidian/reviews/` or `obsidian/workflow/archive/` hits** — those are review/changelog records of the defect and its adjudication, not assertions of it. **DO NOT TOUCH the Palmiter/Ungerstedt loci** — `topics/dopamine-and-the-unified-interface.md` L37/L70/L196/L253, `concepts/motor-selection.md` L102/L111/L279, `archive/concepts/dopamine-selection-interface.md` L44/L61/L277, and `concepts/control-theoretic-will.md` L68/L104 (adjudicated 2026-08-08: sourced to Palmiter via its explicit `motor-selection#The "Can Move / Won't Move" Distinction` anchor link, not to Cai — sound as written). A corpus-wide replace would corrupt correct text. **Length-neutral**; `ai_modified` from a live `date -u`, strictly past; hold `ai_system` — a citation correction is not authoring. Sync after if any body text changes.

Loci 8-10 carry no `Cai` string and were identified by description ("a 2024 *Nature* study" + the can-move/won't-move pair) — confirm on read. **DO NOT TOUCH — the same string is CORRECT here** because Palmiter's DD mice and Ungerstedt's 6-OHDA rats genuinely were dopamine-deficient: `obsidian/topics/dopamine-and-the-unified-interface.md` L37/L70/L196/L253, `obsidian/concepts/motor-selection.md` L102/L111/L279, and `archive/concepts/dopamine-selection-interface.md` L44/L277 — note locus (10) is a **mixed file**: L36 is Cai and defective, L44 is Palmiter and correct. A corpus-wide grep-and-replace on the string would corrupt correct text. **UNRESOLVED, adjudicate on read**: `obsidian/concepts/control-theoretic-will.md` L68 "Gating explains why dopamine-deficient systems retain motor capacity but lose initiative" — the file contains no Cai, Palmiter or Ungerstedt string and I could not determine which source it leans on; do not assume Cai. **THE RATIFICATION MUST BE OVERRIDDEN, NOT DEFERRED TO.** `reviews/deep-review-2026-06-02-interface-specification-programme.md` L36 called it "a defensible compression ... Left as-is", and `workflow/archive/changelog-2026-W23.md` L7200 called it "a fair compression ... left". ChatGPT's 2026-08-07 outer review (L61) verified at primary text and returns "**Materially wrong** ... This passage needs rewriting, not hedging", and `reviews/outer-review-synthesis-2026-08-07.md` L134 records that ChatGPT judged the *empirical description* (then unchecked) while Claude and Gemini judged the *inference*. **A PRECEDENT WORDING ALREADY EXISTS** and was never propagated: `reviews/deep-review-2026-06-02-attention-as-interface.md` L72 rewrote one instance to "mice with impaired phasic dopamine release still move spontaneously but lose reward-oriented vigour — dopamine dynamics modulate the *decision threshold*, not raw motor capacity." ### ✓ 2026-08-08: `phenomenology-of-returning-attention`'s blurb advertises illusionism as defeated — the word appears ONCE in the file, in the description itself, and the body never engages it
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-returning-attention.md
- **Notes**: **Found by the 2026-08-08 17:50 agentic-social blurb check; driver-verified on disk.** **A NEW defect class, orthogonal to the result-verb family** — call it **"unearned second front"**: the blurb names an opponent school the body never addresses.
  **THE DEFECT.** `description:` claims a micro-structure *"that neither materialist **nor illusionist** accounts…"* can explain. **Verified: `illusion` occurs exactly ONCE in the whole file — on line 3, the description itself. `Frankish` 0. `Dennett` 0.** The three accounts the body actually canvasses are neural, threshold and selection.
  **⚠️ WHY THIS IS SHARPER THAN A GENERIC OVER-CLAIM.** Illusionism is the *specific* objection aimed at this article's method. Its entire evidence base is introspective report from trained meditators (*"Trained meditators become expert at observing through introspection… reporting real phenomenological structure rather than culturally constructed narratives"*), and illusionism's core claim is that **introspection systematically misrepresents phenomenal character**. So the blurb advertises the article's most dangerous unaddressed objection as already defeated.
  **⚠️ THE REST OF THE BLURB IS CLEAN — DO NOT "FIX" IT.** Verified: *"reveals"* is body-matched (the body uses it 3x of this structure, and the lead calls the return *"among the most revealing micro-events in conscious life"*); *"micro-structure"* appears twice in the body; and the materialist half mirrors the body's own hedge verbatim — the lead says the phenomenology *"contains a structure that **resists easy materialist** explanation"*. **A verb-shaped sweep would wrongly rewrite a verb this article has earned.**
  **TWO ACCEPTABLE FIXES — pick one, do not split:** (a) **drop "nor illusionist"** from the blurb, leaving the materialist claim the body supports; or (b) **earn it** — add a short body engagement with the introspective-reliability objection, marking it as the framework-boundary point the corpus's own template states (`concepts/qualia.md` L153: *"a representational system need not instantiate what it represents… a framework-boundary point, not an in-framework refutation"*). **(b) is the better article but costs words; (a) is honest and free.** 2571w against a `topics` hard ceiling of 4000, so either fits.
  **THE CLASS IS WORTH A SWEEP LATER, NOT NOW.** Detection is cheap and needs no web budget: for each `description:` naming an opponent school (illusionism, functionalism, eliminativism, panpsychism, physicalism…), grep the **body** for that school and its principal proponents. Zero body hits = unearned. **This is a candidate test, not a defect list** — an article may engage a school without naming it. Do not mint a sweep from a raw count.
  **MIRROR:** sync both trees. `ai_modified` from a live `date -u`, strictly past. **Hold `ai_system`** (`claude-opus-4-6`) — a blurb correction is not authoring.

### ✓ 2026-08-08: `process-content-distinction.md` presents a paraphrase of the Nisbett & Wilson ABSTRACT as a bracketed verbatim quotation — the string occurs 0 times in the 29-page paper, and the bracket edits flip a conjunction to a disjunction
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/process-content-distinction.md
- **Notes**: **Primary-text verified** — full text of Nisbett & Wilson 1977, *Psychological Review* 84(3), 231–259 retrieved (29 pages, 130,674 chars flattened) and searched. § "The Founding Statement" quotes subjects as *"may not be aware of the existence of the stimulus that importantly influenced a response, [or] not aware of the existence of the response, [or] not aware that the stimulus has affected the response."* **`"may not be aware"` occurs 0 times in the paper; `"aware of the existence of the stimulus"` occurs 0 times.** The actual source is the abstract: *"Subjects are sometimes (a) unaware of the existence of a stimulus that importantly influenced a response, (b) unaware of the existence of the response, and (c) unaware that the stimulus has affected the response."*
  Three substitutions ("unaware of" → "may not be aware of"; "a stimulus" → "the stimulus"), plus `(a)/(b)/(c)` replaced by `[or]`. **The last is substantive**: the original is a *conjunction* of three cumulative findings ("and (c)"); the article renders a *disjunction*, weakening the claim it is invoked to support. The editorial brackets make this the severe class — they signal to a reader that everything outside them is verbatim, and nothing outside them is.
  **Fix**: quote the abstract accurately, or convert to indirect speech. ⚠️ **Do NOT touch the article's other Nisbett quotation** — *"people often cannot report accurately on the effects of particular stimuli on higher order, inference-based responses"* is **exactly verbatim** (p. 231, conclusion 1) and verified clean.

### ✓ 2026-08-08: the constitutive-valence thesis never confronts the predictive-processing account that is its leading empirical rival
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/evaluative-phenomenal-character.md
- **Notes**: **From outer review 2026-08-08 (Claude Opus 5). New lens — no open task covers it, and it lands on different files from the seven-task `consciousness-value-connection` pileup.**
  **SECOND FILE, do not drop it**: `obsidian/topics/phenomenal-value-realism.md` carries the same exposure. Handle both or explicitly defer one.
  **THE GAP.** The cluster treats evaluative phenomenal character — the felt badness that *is* badness — as metaphysically basic. The interoceptive-inference / active-inference programme holds that affective valence is **constructed** by hierarchical Bayesian prediction rather than being a primitive constitutive quale. That is the leading empirical account of precisely the property the Map treats as bedrock, and the reviewer reports it is absent across the value and phenomenology clusters.
  **VERIFY THE ABSENCE FIRST — the reviewer's "entirely absent" is a claim, not a measurement.** Grep both files (and `voids/valence-void`, `topics/valence-and-conscious-selection`) for predictive processing, active inference, interoceptive, Seth, Barrett, Friston, Clark before writing anything. **Whose words am I searching for?** — search the vocabulary the articles would use, not only the reviewer's. If engagement already exists somewhere in the cluster, this becomes a propagation task, not a new-content task.
  **CITATIONS — the reviewer's were not verified this session** (WebSearch budget exhausted during processing; only Crossref/SEP lookups were available). Seth & Critchley, "Interoceptive predictive coding," *Trends in Cognitive Sciences* 17(11) (2013): 565–573 and "Barrett, *SCAN* 2017" must be checked at the publisher before citing. Barrett's 2017 *SCAN* paper is the theory-of-constructed-emotion piece; confirm title, authorship and pagination rather than copying the reviewer's shorthand.
  **WHY IT MATTERS.** If valence is a predictive construct, "the felt badness *is* badness" does not obviously survive as a metaphysical identity — the felt quality would be a downstream inferential product rather than a basic evaluative property. This is a genuine risk to the thesis, so it belongs in the article's defeat conditions as well as its body. It interlocks with the open P2 task on the tenet-protective "What Would Challenge This View?" section, which currently lists only conditions the Map has declared non-obtaining.
  **Apply the direct-refutation discipline.** Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** The classification is editor-internal; it belongs in the refine-draft / deep-review changelog entry, not in the article. If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared.
  **A constructivist account of valence is not automatically a defeater** — the Map can hold that prediction explains the *access* to valence without explaining the felt quality itself. But that reply has to be made, not assumed.
  **MIRROR**: sync both trees.

(2009) nor McKemmish et al. (2009).
  **FIVE SIBLINGS ALREADY CARRY THE CALIBRATED VERSION — copy one, do not invent wording**: `concepts/decoherence.md` L95; `topics/motor-control-quantum-zeno.md` L114 (*"Citing Hagan as a settled rebuttal of Tegmark would be selective citation"*); `topics/quantum-biology-and-neural-consciousness.md` L169 (*"one counter-calculation by Hameroff collaborators, unreplicated and still contested—an unresolved dispute, not a settled rebuttal"*); `concepts/coupling-modes.md` L113; `topics/stochastic-emergence-as-quantum-interface-evidence.md` L98.

Same correction, same source siblings.

### ✓ 2026-08-08: `consciousness-value-connection` proves consciousness is NECESSARY for value, then spends the Implications section using it as SUFFICIENT
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/consciousness-value-connection.md
- **Notes**: **From outer review 2026-08-08 (ChatGPT 5.6 Thinking), with independent corroboration on the panpsychism passage from Claude Opus 5.** Synthesis singleton S2. Already at the P1 ceiling. The reviewer's clearest formal charge, and it verifies verbatim on disk.
  **THE DEFECT.** The argument concludes at L50: *"consciousness is necessary for value"* — i.e. Value → Consciousness. Three later passages need the converse:
  - L130 (For Ethics): *"Conscious beings matter; non-conscious systems don't (except instrumentally)."*
  - L150 (For Panpsychism): *"wherever experience goes, value follows."*
  - L148 half-notices the problem — it correctly restates the necessity direction (*"where there is value, there is consciousness"*) two sentences before L150 reverses it.
  None of these follow from necessity. They require consciousness to be **sufficient** for value, which the article never argues.
  **WHY IT MATTERS MOST FOR PANPSYCHISM, and where the second reviewer lands.** Even granting an electron some thin micro-experience, nothing follows about that experience being valenced, meaningful or valuable — as written, the panpsychism paragraph establishes roughly the converse of the thesis it presents itself as illustrating. **Claude independently attacked the same paragraph from the other side**: if panpsychism is true and the connection still holds, the thesis becomes *"vacuous as a criterion of moral discrimination, since it no longer distinguishes any system from any other."* **Two different complaints about one paragraph are not convergence** — but they are two independent reasons that paragraph needs rewriting, and the repair should answer both.
  **FIX — separate the theses rather than hedging the sentences.** The article currently runs four distinct claims together and should mark each with its own argument and confidence: (a) phenomenal states can instantiate intrinsic value; (b) all intrinsic value is phenomenal; (c) consciousness suffices for moral status; (d) value qua value is causally efficacious. (a) is well supported; (b) is the exclusivity thesis, owned by the merged rivals task; (c) is the sufficiency step at issue here; (d) is locus 3 of the hub/satellite task. Splitting them makes the bridge premises visible instead of letting one plausible thesis license three ambitious ones.
  **THE VULCAN IS THE SHARP TEST OF (c), and it interlocks with the merged rivals task.** Shepherd, "Sentience, Vulcans, and zombies," *AI & Society* (2024), DOI `10.1007/s00146-023-01835-6` — verified — asks whether *phenomenality* or *valenced* phenomenality does the moral work. A conscious-but-affectless subject is a live counterexample to L130 as written. Cite it once, in whichever task runs first; do not cite it twice.
  **DO NOT retreat to vagueness.** The Map is entitled to hold that conscious beings have moral status — it just has to argue the sufficiency step, or mark it as a further commitment, rather than presenting it as a corollary.
  **⚠️ LENGTH — SHARED BUDGET.** Article is 2684 body words against a 3500 hard ceiling, shared across four merged tasks. **This task must be length-neutral**: separating the four theses is a restructuring of existing sentences, and the L130 / L146–150 rewrites replace text rather than adding to it. Decompose before trusting `analyze_length`.
  **MIRROR**: live at `hugo/content/concepts/consciousness-value-connection.md`. Sync both trees. `ai_modified` from a live `date -u`, strictly past.

### ✓ 2026-08-08: the article's main anti-illusionist rebuttal is a question Frankish poses and answers IN THE PAPER the article cites — verified at the primary text, and both reviewers reached it independently
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/consciousness-value-connection.md
- **Notes**: **From convergent outer reviews (2026-08-08, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude].** Synthesis cluster 4. Already at the P1 ceiling; the convergence is recorded rather than used for an upgrade. Highest-value externally-verified finding of the cycle — checked at primary source during processing, not taken on either reviewer's word.
  **THE DEFECT.** L112 answers illusionism with the audience regress: *"For the illusion to occur, something must be deceived. Who is having the illusion?"* — presented as severe and unanswered.
  **VERIFIED**: Frankish (2016), which this article lists as **reference 5**, contains a section titled **"3.3. Who is the audience?"**. Frankish raises the identical objection (*"An illusion presupposes an audience. Who is the audience for the illusion of phenomenal consciousness?"*), rejects the Cartesian-theatre reading the regress depends on, and answers: *"There need be no unified audience for the illusion smaller than the organism as a whole (or at least its central nervous system)."* Confirmed in Frankish's own eprint PDF; the section heading and quotations above are exact.
  So the principal rebuttal attacks a position its own cited source explicitly disavows, and the regress lands only against an opponent no illusionist is.
  **CLAUDE REACHED THE SAME PLACE INDEPENDENTLY**: *"a bare regress argument… that (a) Frankish has directly answered (seeming = misrepresentation, no inner audience required) and (b) does not engage the normative-challenge literature at all."* Claude adds that Frankish's own *Neuroethics* 17(2) (2024) piece — DOI `10.1007/s12152-024-09562-5`, **verified** — holds that illusionism *preserves* ethical value rather than destroying it, which the article's framing assumes it cannot.
  **WHAT SURVIVES — DO NOT TOUCH IT.** Both reviewers credit the repair already made at L116: the practical/intrinsic "mattering" distinction. Keep it. Only the audience regress at L112–114 is at issue.
  **⚠️ GEMINI ATTACKED THIS SAME SECTION AND WAS WRONG — read this before you touch L116.** Its headline finding quotes a concession — *"Whether we call it phenomenal or quasi-phenomenal, it plays the value-grounding role"* — and calls it "the total concession of the eliminativist and physicalist position." That string greps to **zero live articles**: it survives only inside `reviews/pessimistic-2026-07-16-consciousness-value-connection.md` and its Hugo twin, where the Map's own review quotes the **pre-fix** L116 and recommends the repair that landed 2026-07-16. **Gemini read our published correction as our current position.** If a later pass sees that quote resurface as a "finding," it is this artefact.
  **THE BETTER LINE, proposed by both reviewers**: Kammerer's normative challenge — asking the illusionist what becomes of the apparent consciousness/value relation — presses illusionism from exactly the domain this article is about, and is dialectically far stronger than the audience regress. **Kammerer, "The Normative Challenge for Illusionist Views of Consciousness," *Ergo* 6 (2019), DOI `10.3998/ergo.12405314.0006.032` — verified exact.** *House of Mirrors* (Oxford) is verified by title only; check any internal argument names at the text.
  **FIX — Apply the direct-refutation discipline.** Identify what kind of engagement the issue calls for: showing the opponent's position is defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move the framework has not earned by its own standards, or honestly marking the framework-boundary disagreement. Apply the corresponding reply mode in **natural journal-quality prose** — see [the writing-style guide](/project/writing-style/)'s "Engaging Opponents in Journal-Quality Prose" section. **Do not expose mode labels in the article body.** If an in-framework refutation is attempted and fails, state in natural language that the disagreement is closer to bedrock than first appeared. The substantive target is the **non-phenomenal account of seeming and representation**; as written the rebuttal reduces to "illusionism is false because genuine seeming is phenomenal," which is what illusionism denies.

### ✓ 2026-08-08: `consciousness-value-connection` settles its disputed premise by definition and engages no non-experiential rival — all three same-day reviewers, independently
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/consciousness-value-connection.md

They were four symptoms of one gap, and **one rival-engagement rewrite discharges all four**. Four separate passes would quadruple the length cost on an article already in `soft_warning`. Do not re-split them.
  **THE ONE DEFECT, in three reviewers' words.** ChatGPT: *"the controversial premise is hidden in the definition of mattering… it defines 'value' so that objective value is excluded before the argument begins."* Claude: Step 1 and Step 2 *"jointly entail the conclusion only if 'mattering' is already read phenomenally in Step 2"*, and the "Note on functional interests" (L48) *"simply restates Step 2."* Gemini: *"a definitional loop… logically excludes any objective list theory, non-experiential desire-satisfaction theory, or biocentric theory of value from the outset."* All three quoted spans verify verbatim on disk (L44, L46, L48, L84–86).
  **MEASURED ABSENCE, not the reviewers' word.** Grep of the live article returns **zero** for every one of: Moore, fitting-attitude, buck-passing, objective list, desire-satisfaction, Benatar, Shepherd, Kriegel, Kammerer, Vulcan, Carruthers, Mogensen, Bradford, Parfit, Feldman, Korsgaard, Kagan, Mackie, Street, Birch. The only mind-independence rival engaged anywhere is the Platonic Form of the Good (L92–98) — the weakest available form of the view. L58 then asserts the verdict on Moore's own thought experiment without engaging it: *"A beautiful sunset in a universe without observers has no value—not even aesthetic value."*
  **THE RIVALS, by family — each reviewer named a different one, which is why the gap rather than any single rival is the convergent object:**
  * **Aesthetic / comparative objective value (ChatGPT).** **VERIFIED at primary text** — Moore, *Principia Ethica* (1903), replying to Sidgwick: *"Let us imagine one world exceedingly beautiful… And then imagine the ugliest world you can possibly conceive… The only thing we are not entitled to imagine is that any human being ever has or ever, by any possibility, can, live in either."* Moore argues the beautiful world is still better in itself. This attacks the disputed premise directly and posits no causally active abstract object, so the Platonism response does not touch it.
  * **Non-conscious welfare subjects (Claude).** Carruthers, "Suffering Without Subjectivity," *Phil. Studies* 121(2) (2004); Mogensen, "Desire-fulfilment and consciousness," *Phil. Studies* 183(2) (2025): 511–529; Bradford, "Consciousness and welfare subjectivity," *Noûs* 57(4) (2022): 905–921 — **Bradford's 2022 volume/year pairing was checked at Crossref and is correct**, despite vol. 57 usually reading 2023. Note the article's "What About Unconscious Value?" objection (L118–124) addresses unconscious states *in a conscious subject* — a different question — and never confronts a welfare subject with no capacity for consciousness at all.
  * **Attitudinal hedonism and agency-based status (Claude).** Feldman, *Pleasure and the Good Life* (OUP 2004) grounds welfare in being-pleased-*that*, threatening the evaluative-qualia machinery directly; Korsgaard, *Fellow Creatures* (2018) and Kagan, *How to Count Animals* (2019) ground standing in agency rather than valenced experience. L134's *"the question shifts from 'Is it intelligent?' to 'Is there something it is like to be it?'"* presupposes sentientism against them.
  * **Fitting-attitude / buck-passing (ChatGPT)** — evaluative objectivity without any Platonic commitment.
  * **Objective-list theories (all three).** ⚠️ **Gemini's supporting source is unusable**: "Caleb Alabd 2025" was not found and no matching philosopher exists. **Do not cite it.** The lens is corroborated by the other two reviewers with verified sources; take the rival from them.
  **SECOND, DISTINCT CONFLATION — "good for."** L48 and L86 claim nothing can be good or bad *for* a non-conscious system. **Benatar, "Sentience and Why It Matters," *Cambridge Quarterly of Healthcare Ethics* 34(4) (2025; online April 2026)** — verified at the publisher — reaches a **sentience-friendly** conclusion while explicitly rejecting that move: he distinguishes functional, biotic and conscious interests, allows that things can objectively be good or bad for a non-conscious plant or car, and argues separately that such interests are the wrong kind to confer moral standing. **Use 2025**, not ChatGPT's "Benatar 2026."
  **THE DEFEAT CONDITIONS ARE THE SAME DEFECT, which is why they merge here (3/3 convergent, three different routes).** L176 requires experience-independent value be **"demonstrated"**; L180 requires abstract value to exist **"as a force like gravity"**; L182 closes **"The connection stands."** ChatGPT calls it a burden asymmetry — the standard demanded of rivals exceeds the introspective and abductive standard the Map itself met, and the gravity test confuses normative reality with efficient causation. Each is a real epistemic risk the Map runs. **And soften the closer** to something like *"the connection remains the Map's working hypothesis"*, matching the apex's own conditional framing.
  **THE REFERENCE LIST IS THE THIRD FACE OF THE SAME GAP (2/3 convergent — ChatGPT and Claude, on all three names).** The References (L205–210) hold exactly six items, and **Wolf, Metz, Nagel, Frankish and Chalmers are cited nowhere in the body** — they appear only in the bibliography, so a bare list implies endorsement.

### ✓ 2026-08-07: the `description:`-overclaim family is measured but only a third swept — 19 verb-form candidates unread and a 64-file noun-form axis never touched
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/emotion-and-dualism.md
- **Notes**: **THIS IS THE SCOPED RESIDUE OF A FAMILY THAT HAS BEEN WORKED THREE TIMES AND IS STILL OPEN. Six blurbs are fixed; the population was never fully enumerated.** Fixed so far, do **not** redo: `quantum-immortality-and-the-quantum-suicide-survival-argument`, `psychedelics-and-the-filter-model` (18:25Z), and `many-worlds`, `split-brain-consciousness`, `inverted-qualia`, `atemporal-causation` (22:24Z).
  **WHY IT KEEPS RECURRING — the structural cause, worth stating once.** `refine-draft`, `deep-review` and `condense` all operate on **bodies**; none re-reads `description:`. So a blurb is a fossil of whatever the article claimed when it was written, and it is simultaneously the **highest-leverage surface in the corpus** — the meta description served to search engines and social cards, and the first thing an LLM fetching the page reads.
  **AXIS 1 — VERB FORM, PARTIALLY SWEPT.** Pattern: `grep -rnE '^description: .*(reverses|reversing|dissolves|dissolving|refutes|refuting|establishes|establishing|proves|proving|demonstrates|demonstrating|vindicates|confirms|settles|decisively|shows that)' --include=*.md obsidian/ archive/` — ~40 hits, **13 read, 4 defective (31%)**. **19 named candidates remain unread**: live — `positions/finding-level-calibration`, `topics/the-hard-problem-in-non-western-philosophy`, `project/project`, `voids/witnessing-void`, `voids/self-transcendence-void`, `concepts/occasionalism`, `concepts/self-reference-paradox`; archive — `contemplative-neuroscience-integration`, `perceptual-fidelity-and-the-interface`, `phenomenological-method-and-evidence-standards`, `computational-cognitive-limits`, `quantum-biology`, `retrocausal-selection`, `phenomenology-of-flow-states`, `lucid-dreaming-and-consciousness`, `unobservable-self`, `analytic-idealism`, `mental-imagery`.
  **AXIS 2 — NOUN FORM, MEASURED BUT ENTIRELY UNSWEPT, and this is where the family started.** The psychedelics blurb that opened all of this overclaimed via *"provide pharmacological evidence for"* — **no verb in Axis 1 matches it.** Scanning `^description:` for `evidence for|evidence that|support for|case for|demonstration of|proof of|proof that|confirmation of|vindication of` across `obsidian/` + `archive/` returns **64 files: 28 live articles, 29 archive, 7 in positions/reviews**. Largely **disjoint** from Axis 1. Ranked live candidates from that scan: **`topics/emotion-and-dualism`** (*"the strongest empirical case for dualism… transforms the philosophy of emotion"*), **`topics/pain-consciousness-and-causal-power`** (*"epiphenomenalism made untenable"*), `concepts/phenomenology-of-choice-and-volition` (*"each phase providing irreducible evidence"*), `topics/clinical-phenomenology-and-altered-experience`, `concepts/neurophenomenology-and-contemplative-neuroscience`, `topics/dream-consciousness`.
  **⚠️ THAT IS A CANDIDATE LIST, NOT A DEFECT LIST.** Base rate on the read sample is **31%**, so expect roughly two-thirds to be sound. **Read each body before touching its blurb.** The non-defect rule established across three passes: **a blurb that ATTRIBUTES a strong claim to an opponent or rival theory is reporting it, not asserting it** — that is not a defect.
  **THE FIX STANDARD — gain precision, do not merely hedge.** The best repairs name the position *and* its limit in one sentence: *"one-branch collapse never generates it — no dialectical advantage for dualism"*; *"Unity of agency survives; unity of experience remains undetermined."* Several bodies state objections **sharper** than their blurbs; the blurb should get sharper too. `concepts/retrocausality` L3 is the model for a conditional (*"On retrocausal interpretations, …"*).
  **SCOPE THIS RUN**: take the two ranked live candidates named above plus as many of the 19 verb-form unread as fit. **Report what you did not reach** rather than implying closure — every pass so far has done that, which is why the family is still tractable.
  **DESCRIPTION LENGTH**: CLAUDE.md says 150–160 chars; the corpus median is **176** and p90 **233**, and the six repaired blurbs run 188–244 because each carries a claim and its limit. Do not compress a correct blurb to hit the documented figure — that doc-vs-corpus gap is its own open question.
  **MIRROR**: all live. Sync both trees. `ai_modified` from a live `date -u`, **strictly past** (five forks have future-dated today). **Hold `ai_system`** — correcting a blurb to match its body is not authoring. Bodies are out of scope: `concepts/sleep-and-consciousness` asserts *"This proves the interface is trainable"* in its own body voice, so its blurb is faithful; whether the body overclaims is a different lens and a different task.

  **1. `obsidian/concepts/many-worlds.md` L3 — CONTRADICTED, and the strongest case.** Blurb: *"Why the Map rejects MWI: it raises unanswerable questions about indexical identity, **dissolves probability's meaning**, and fails to explain consciousness."* Two of its three grounds are retracted verbatim in the body. L81: *"The objection is therefore **not** 'MWI cannot accommodate indexical singularity'—**it can, branch-locally**—but 'MWI cannot simultaneously accommodate branch-local indexical singularity and the cross-branch personal-identity relation its Born-rule derivation requires.'"* L107: *"the Map's position is **not** that MWI *cannot* recover the Born rule but that the recoveries it has produced depend on identity and probability principles the Map rejects."* Only "fails to explain consciousness" survives untouched.   **2. `obsidian/topics/split-brain-consciousness.md` L3 — OVERSTATED.** Blurb closes *"Unity **proves** remarkably resilient."* L78: the Pinto data *"establishes a unity of *agency*, not a unity of *experience*… so the resilience argument earns its dualist conclusion only at the experience level — **precisely where the evidence stays underdetermined**."* The L142 section heading is literally *"The Evidence Settles Neither Side"*. The blurb's earlier *"the evidence suggests"* clause is properly hedged and should stay; only the unhedged final sentence needs replacing.
  **3. `obsidian/concepts/inverted-qualia.md` L3 — OVERSTATED.** Blurb: *"—**proving** function doesn't fix phenomenology."* L175: *"What survives is the thought experiment's *conceptual* force: strict behavioural identity plus qualia-difference is a limit case the Map treats as **physically unrealised** though conceivable."* Conceptual force is not proof.

L91: *"The non-violation claim depends on the selection operating strictly within the probability space that physics leaves open, **which is the framework's design constraint rather than a demonstrated result**."* The Libet-dissolution clause is sound (L193 affirms it). `obsidian/concepts/retrocausality.md` L3 is the pattern to copy — its *"On retrocausal interpretations"* conditional is exactly what this blurb lacks.
  **Checked and found CLEAN** (recorded so they are not re-litigated): `concepts/epiphenomenalism`, `concepts/sleep-and-consciousness` (L106 asserts *"This proves the interface is trainable"* in the body's own voice, so the blurb matches — whether the *body* overclaims is a different lens), `topics/wanting-liking-and-the-value-in-mechanism-fork`, `topics/the-consequence-argument-for-incompatibilism`, `topics/overdetermination-dissolution-under-selection-only-interactionism`, `concepts/multi-mind-collapse-problem`, `concepts/retrocausality`, `topics/the-steelman-for-process-monism`, `topics/enactivism-challenge-to-interactionist-dualism`. Non-defect rule applied throughout: a blurb that **attributes** a strong claim to an opponent or a rival theory is reporting it, not asserting it.
  **WHERE THE SWEEP STOPPED — do not read this task as closing the family.** Candidates came from `grep -rnE '^description: .*(reverses|reversing|dissolves|dissolving|refutes|refuting|establishes|establishing|proves|proving|demonstrates|demonstrating|vindicates|confirms|settles|decisively|shows that)' --include=*.md obsidian/ archive/`, which returned about forty lines; thirteen were read. **Not read**: `positions/finding-level-calibration`, `topics/the-hard-problem-in-non-western-philosophy`, `project/project`, `voids/witnessing-void`, `voids/self-transcendence-void`, `concepts/occasionalism`, `concepts/self-reference-paradox`, and twelve `archive/` descriptions (`contemplative-neuroscience-integration`, `perceptual-fidelity-and-the-interface`, `phenomenological-method-and-evidence-standards`, `computational-cognitive-limits`, `quantum-biology`, `retrocausal-selection`, `phenomenology-of-flow-states`, `lucid-dreaming-and-consciousness`, `unobservable-self`, `analytic-idealism`, `mental-imagery`). The `reviews/` hits are records and out of scope. **The verb list is itself the sweep's blind spot**: the psychedelics blurb that started all this overclaimed through the *noun* phrase "provide pharmacological evidence for", which no verb in that pattern matches. A second pass should search noun-form assertions — `evidence for`, `support for`, `case for`, `demonstration of` — as well as verbs.

### ✓ 2026-08-07: [P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3) records the Map's agreement with Rawlette in hedonistic terms and carries a shift-condition the Map's own sublime article asserts is satisfied — phenomenal value **pluralism** is in six live articles and no register entry
- **Type**: positions-evolve
- **File**: obsidian/positions/value-in-selection.md
- **Notes**: **REGISTER GAP WITH A LIVE INTERNAL TENSION, NOT A FLAT CONTRADICTION.** `obsidian/positions/value-in-selection.md` **[P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3)** ("Intrinsic value resides in phenomenal experience") states the position in **Rawlette's hedonistic vocabulary** — *"the felt badness of suffering is badness, the felt goodness of joy is goodness — one property under two descriptions"*, carried by *"coherence, transparency, explanatory economy (Rawlette, The Feeling of Value, 2016)"* — and its **Would shift if** line ends: *"or careful phenomenology showed goodness separable from pleasure in direct experience."* **That is precisely what the Map's own article argues obtains, and treats as strengthening rather than shifting.** `obsidian/topics/the-sublime-and-negative-aesthetics.md` **L42**: *"Their existence is the strongest single case for phenomenal value pluralism: the claim that experience carries several distinct, irreducible dimensions of intrinsic value, of which hedonic pleasure is only one."* **L82** names the departure explicitly: *"Rawlette defends the hedonistic version: pleasure and pain exhaust intrinsic value. The Map extends the position to phenomenal value pluralism."* **L86**: the negative categories *"break the correlation while preserving the aesthetic value, isolating the aesthetic dimension from the hedonic one the way pain asymbolia isolates the sensory component of pain from its affective component."* **PROPAGATION — the pluralism claim is held across at least six live articles and zero register entries**: `obsidian/topics/phenomenal-value-realism.md` L120 (*"The Map extends this to phenomenal value pluralism"*), `obsidian/topics/the-sublime-and-negative-aesthetics.md` L42/L82, `obsidian/topics/ethics-under-dualism.md` L76, `obsidian/apex/living-with-the-map.md` L72, `obsidian/topics/purpose-and-alignment.md` L161, `obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-moral-life.md` L87. A grep for "pluralism" across all of `obsidian/positions/*.md` returns **zero** hits. **WHAT TO DO**: either extend [P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3)'s **Asserts** to record that the Map holds the *pluralist* extension (several irreducible value dimensions — hedonic, aesthetic, eudaimonic, autonomous, epistemic, relational — not Rawlette's hedonic-exhaustive version), and correspondingly narrow the shift-condition so it no longer reads as satisfied by the Map's own position; or mint a sibling entry for pluralism with its own calibration and leave [P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3) as the bare realism claim. **Do not simply delete the shift-condition** — under a strictly hedonistic reading of [P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3) it is the right condition, and the honest content is that the Map departed from Rawlette on exactly this axis without the register recording the departure. Calibration should not exceed [P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3)'s own (credence moderate · external-evidence grade D · argued phenomenologically); pluralism is a *broader* claim than the hedonic identity and its support is the same phenomenological kind, so it does not earn a higher band. Note the domain scope: this register is the value-blind/value-sensitive fork and [P-VS3](/positions/value-in-selection/#p-vs3) is its "numbered last, logically first" given — check whether a pluralism entry belongs here or wants a home closer to the ethics/metaethics cluster. `positions/` is at 14/80. ### ✓ 2026-08-07: `aesthetics-and-consciousness` L92 attributes an aesthetic-residue thesis to Byrne & Hilbert, who wrote a defence of colour PHYSICALISM — same lens, same article, as the Revonsuo locus just fixed
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/aesthetics-and-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: Found 2026-08-07 by the refine-draft that fixed the Revonsuo misattribution at L128 in this same article; **flagged rather than acted on, deliberately, because it needs verification at the source and a corpus-internal read is not sufficient**. Do that verification first.
  **THE LOCUS (read on disk).** L92: *"Colours have aesthetic quality independent of what they represent—a particular blue is *cool* or *warm*, *deep* or *shallow* (**Byrne & Hilbert, 2003, show that colour science fully specifies the physical properties without capturing the aesthetic ones**)."* Reference at L189: *"Byrne, A. & Hilbert, D. (2003). 'Color Realism and Color Science.' *Behavioral and Brain Sciences*, 26(1), 3–21."*
  **WHY IT LOOKS WRONG.** That BBS target article is a **defence of colour physicalism** — its thesis is that colours *are* physical properties (types of surface spectral reflectance) and that colour science does not undermine colour realism. Recruiting it to show that colour science *fails to capture* something is the same failure mode as the Revonsuo locus nine lines of argument away: **a physicalist enlisted as the authority for a residue the Map infers.** There is a second possibility worth testing separately — that they make **no aesthetic claim at all**, in which case the sentence attributes a thesis they never advanced rather than merely mis-framing one they did.
  **CORROBORATION — L92 is the odd one out.** Narrowed grep for the colour pair across `obsidian/` + `archive/` (beware: a bare `Hilbert` grep matches *Hilbert space* across the quantum articles and is useless): the corpus's other substantive uses cite them for **colour-space structural asymmetries constraining inversion** — `archive/topics/aesthetic-dimension-of-consciousness` L136 and `archive/topics/consciousness-and-aesthetic-experience-as-philosophical-evidence` L66 — which is a plausible and different claim. `concepts/first-order-representationalism` L142 carries them in its reference list with no in-text claim. **No sibling repeats L92's framing, so this is a single locus, not a family.**
  **VERIFY AT THE SOURCE BEFORE EDITING.** The BBS target article is widely available; check what they actually claim about colour science's completeness and whether aesthetic properties appear at all. Do not settle this from the corpus or from an aggregator — and exclude unfinishablemap.org from any search, since our own page will rank for the phrasing.
  **THEN FIX BY RE-FRAMING, NOT DELETING** (per the Revonsuo precedent at L128, now live): if they defend colour physicalism, cite them for *that* and make the aesthetic-residue step explicitly the Map's own inference. The aesthetic point stands on its own; it just must not borrow their authority. If they make no aesthetic claim whatever, drop the attribution from the parenthetical and keep the claim in the Map's voice.
  **LENGTH — DO NOT TRUST `analyze_length` HERE.** It reports 3122/3000 `soft_warning`, but that counts the reference apparatus. Decomposed: References 188 + Further Reading 281 → **prose 2653/3000, ~347 words of headroom.** Write the clause the fix needs.
  **MIRROR**: live at `hugo/content/topics/aesthetics-and-consciousness.md`. Sync after editing. `ai_modified` from a live `date -u`, strictly past. **Hold `ai_system`** — re-framing a citation is correction, not authoring.

### ✓ 2026-08-07: `aesthetics-and-consciousness` L128 recruits Revonsuo — a biological naturalist — as support for "phenomenal unity poses problems for materialism"; the coalesce added the cite to a previously bare claim
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/aesthetics-and-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: **CITATION-FRAMING, NOT METADATA — the metadata is correct and a prior review verified it.** `obsidian/topics/aesthetics-and-consciousness.md` **L128** reads: *"If [phenomenal unity](/concepts/unity-of-consciousness/) in seeing a red apple already poses problems for materialism (Revonsuo, 2006), the unity of experiencing beauty—that same six-domain binding, carrying intrinsic value—poses those problems in amplified form."* Revonsuo is a **biological naturalist**; the article's own reference list at **L198** prints the book's subtitle — *"Revonsuo, A. (2006). Inner Presence: Consciousness as a Biological Phenomenon. MIT Press"* — which contradicts the framing on the page that cites it. Revonsuo articulates the BP2 unity puzzle as a hard problem **for consciousness science**, not as trouble for materialism. **The rest of the corpus frames him correctly**, which is why this is a single-locus defect and not a family: `obsidian/topics/lucid-dreaming-and-dualist-rendering.md` L48 ("[l]eading **physicalist** dream researchers — Hobson, Metzinger, Revonsuo"); `obsidian/research/lucid-dreaming-mind-side-rendering-2026-03-09.md` L85 ("Revonsuo's evolutionary framing is physicalist"); `obsidian/research/consciousness-dreams-2026-01-14.md` L132 ("Revonsuo's naturalism differs from site's dualism"); `obsidian/topics/quantum-holism-and-phenomenal-unity.md` L52 and `obsidian/project/cluster-integration-discipline.md` L59 cite him for the BP1/BP2 **taxonomy** only, which is neutral and correct. **PROVENANCE**: the ancestor `archive/topics/binding-and-beauty.md` **L119** (one of the four articles in this hub's `coalesced_from`) makes the identical claim with **no citation at all** — the coalesce attached an authority to a bare assertion and attached the wrong kind. **WHY IT SURVIVED**: `obsidian/reviews/deep-review-2026-07-18-unity-of-consciousness.md` L50 names this exact locus as "**Deliberately NOT changed**" because it "correctly cite[s] Revonsuo's genuine *2006 book* Inner Presence" — a correct metadata verdict on a question that was not the framing question. Per citation-framing-accuracy-lens and quote-fidelity-defects-survive-metadata-reviews. **FIX**: **RE-FRAME, DO NOT DELETE.** The BP2 unity puzzle is real and Revonsuo did articulate it; recast the parenthetical so it credits him with the *distinction* (the unity of experience is a distinct explanandum from feature-binding — Revonsuo 1999/2006) rather than with the anti-materialist verdict, and let the "poses problems for materialism" step be the Map's own inference. **THREE-TREE SWEEP DONE**: the defective string `problems for materialism` appears at `obsidian/topics/aesthetics-and-consciousness.md` L128, `hugo/content/topics/aesthetics-and-consciousness.md` **L133 (LIVE PUBLISHED PAGE)**, `archive/topics/binding-and-beauty.md` L119, `hugo/content/archive/topics/binding-and-beauty.md` L123. The two `binding-and-beauty` loci are the **uncited** ancestor form — a weaker, separate issue (bare assertion, no misattribution); fix the hub first, and note the archived pages assert it flat. Per obsidian-only-fix-leaves-defect-live-in-hugo, **sync after editing** so the published page does not keep the defect for ~3h. Article is 48w over its soft threshold — keep the fix length-neutral. Found by optimistic review 2026-08-07; see [optimistic-2026-08-07-aesthetics-wing](/reviews/optimistic-2026-08-07-aesthetics-wing/).

Both loci below read on disk.
  **LOCUS 1 — this file, L3, and the verb is not merely overclaimed but MISASSIGNED.** The description ends *"…and how **the Map's one-branch collapse realism dissolves it**."* The body at **L78** says the opposite twice over: *"The anti-immortality conclusion is available to a single-world physicalist who couples objective collapse with a non-reductionist account of persons; it **does not require, and so does not establish, the Map's dualism**. The description takes the Everettian's verb and hands it to the Map. **Rewrite it to the body's actual verdict**: the immortality expectation forms only under branch-relative identity; on the Map's one-branch reading it never arises; and that is a difference of explanatory route, not a dialectical advantage. Look for `description:` fields that assert a dualist advantage, a refutation, or a "reverses/dissolves/refutes/establishes" verb, and check each against the body's current conclusion. **Report what you find as evidence** (what you ran, what it returned) rather than asserting a count; a bare grep over `description:` will over-match, so read before flagging. - **Type**: refine-draft

L43, nine lines later, classifies the same device the other way: "**Proportional and PID controllers.** A thermostat outputs a corrective signal **proportional** to temperature error; an industrial PID controller **adds integral** and derivative terms." The lead L27 sides with L37 ("Integral feedback, robust (near-perfect) adaptation ... fully realised end to end in systems no one credits with experience — **a thermostat**, an operational-amplifier integrator"), and so does L55, which is where it costs something: the article's central reusable primitive is named "**The thermostat floor**" and runs "Integral control is implemented in devices that plainly feel nothing. If the *same* control property carried phenomenality, **the thermostat would qualify**". So the standing reductio the article offers the rest of the corpus rests on a premise its own ladder denies. **TWO REPAIR DIRECTIONS — adjudicate, do not assume.** (a) L43 is the error: a classic on/off bang-bang mechanical thermostat does drive steady-state error to zero, which is the adaptation property at issue, whereas a purely proportional controller leaves a standing offset — on this reading L43's classification is corrected and the floor stands as written. (b) L27/L37/L55 over-credit the device: if the thermostat is proportional/bang-bang and not an integrator, the floor should be re-anchored on the **op-amp integrator** the article already has at L44 ("can implement the integral term *as such* — the very operation Yi et al. identify in the bacterium"), which requires renaming the primitive and rewording L55's reductio. Either way one consistent story must survive; a fix that leaves L43 and L55 disagreeing has not closed this. **DO NOT TOUCH L69 and L75** — those use the thermostat only as the rival's no-stake foil ("no stake, no body that can die, no self-model" / "richer than a thermostat's, but the same *kind* of thing") and are sound under either repair. **LENGTH**: 2195 words, `ok` (concepts soft 2500) — room for either fix, no condensation pressure.

### ✓ 2026-08-07: `topics/anti-correlation-probes-for-ai-consciousness` L90 grants an AI "first-person access to the mind-arena" — the arena is a domain of outcomes, and the source article says access to a conscious system
- **Type**: refine-draft

- **Notes**: **FLOOR-RESTORING MINT** by interactive /replenish-queue 2026-08-07T~13:3xZ (standard; same pre-count of **2** as the sibling mint above; no open task touches this file). **THE DEFECT, verified at all three loci this session.** `obsidian/topics/anti-correlation-probes-for-ai-consciousness.md` **L90** reads: *"the [dualism-as-AI-risk-mitigation](/topics/dualism-as-ai-risk-mitigation/) argument turns on whether an advanced AI has genuine first-person access to the mind-arena"*. That is a sense-mismatch, and it erases the premise the same sentence depends on. `obsidian/concepts/mind-arena.md` **L33** defines the arena as *"the domain of outcomes that pass through minds"* and states explicitly that *"It names a **territory**, not a mechanism"*; **L45** settles the canonical reading as the *domain of outcomes*. A domain of outcomes is not the sort of thing anything has first-person access **to**. The source article the clause is reporting, `obsidian/topics/dualism-as-ai-risk-mitigation.md` **L128**, actually says an ensouled AI *"would have first-person access to the same kind of system whose third-person opacity grounds the shield"* — first-person access to a **conscious system**, not to the arena. **WHY IT IS NOT COSMETIC**: L130 of the source makes exactly that gap load-bearing (*"Even an ensouled AI's first-person access does not obviously translate into third-person predictive access to **other minds**; the shield's load-bearing claim is about predictive access from outside"*), and that gap is what licenses the *"survives only partially"* conclusion **which L90's own clause leans on**. Collapsing access-to-a-conscious-system into access-to-the-arena silently deletes the inside/outside distinction that makes the partial-survival verdict come out. **DO NOT ADD A `[[mind-arena]]` WIKILINK HERE.** Linking the phrase would ratify the conflation rather than repair it — this is the navigation-surfaces-carry-unreviewed-claims failure mode in miniature, where the fix belongs to the wording and not to the link. **THE FIX** is to re-word L90 so the access predicate takes a conscious system as its object, tracking L128: something of the form *"turns on whether an advanced AI has genuine first-person access to the same kind of conscious system whose third-person opacity grounds the shield"*. Preserve the rest of the paragraph verbatim — the three-way *constitutive / correlated / orthogonal* underdetermination framing and the *"survives only partially"* clause are both already correctly calibrated. **SCOPE CHECK BEFORE EDITING**: grep `mind-arena` across `obsidian/`, `archive/` and `hugo/content/` and report whether any other article commits the same access-to-a-domain slip; fix only loci you verify, and state plainly which you checked and did not find rather than implying absence. **LENGTH-NEUTRAL** — this is a clause rewrite. **ATTRIBUTION**: hold this file's own `ai_system`. (2025), "The unbearable slowness of being: Why do we live at 10 bits/s?", *Neuron* 113(2), 192–204, DOI 10.1016/j.neuron.2024.11.008, PMID 39694032** — confirmed at PubMed this session.
