### ✓ 2026-08-04: repair four source-characterisation defects in `phenomenology-of-memory-and-the-self`, including a reversed Tippett finding and two authors named in prose but missing from the references
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-memory-and-the-self.md
- **Notes**: **From convergent outer reviews (2026-08-04, 2/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude]** — for item (4), the Tippett gloss, which is the highest-value item here. ChatGPT called the article's use "fair at a high level, but insufficiently granular"; Claude was blunter — "This over-cleans the source." Verification at the publisher went further than either and found a reversal. Items (1)-(3) are ChatGPT singletons and are unaffected by the convergence. Substance below unchanged. All four verified 2026-08-04; the article's citations are *real* (the 27 July pass cleaned that up) but several are compressed or over-read. (1) **Hurlburt and Petitmengin are named at L96 and appear nowhere in the References section (L178-206)** — confirmed by direct check, a mechanically detectable defect. Add full references for Descriptive Experience Sampling and the microphenomenological interview. While there, the claim that these methods "restrict the confabulation-eligible space" (verbatim, L96) is an uncited synthesis: it should not be allowed to imply that disciplined *reporting* validates the historical accuracy of remembered content. The article's own "whether the restriction reaches access is methodologically undetermined" already concedes this — make the scope explicit at the point of claim. (2) **Husserl-Tulving bridge, L60**: "Husserl identified the temporal structure of retention; Tulving named its cognitive form". Husserlian *retention* is the primal holding-on to the just-elapsed phase of ongoing experience; Husserl distinguishes it from free *recollection* of an absent past event. Tulving's autonoetic episodic remembering is closer to recollection. Replace the implied identification with a qualified analogy. (3) **Patient RB, L72**: the article already hedges well ("at minimum a separable factor", "Whether the separable factor is a phenomenal property or a functional self-attentional tag is contested"), so this is a light touch — add that the case is interpretively contested at the source, citing **Roache, R. (2016), "Memory and mineness in personal identity", *Philosophical Psychology* 29(4), 479-489, DOI 10.1080/09515089.2015.1102216** (verified real; Roache argues Klein and Nichols's stronger characterisation of RB is not adequately supported). Roache currently appears nowhere in the corpus. Mark it n=1 and hypothesis-generating. (4) **Tippett et al., L94**: disaggregate — amnestic MCI and Alzheimer's differed, continuity *beliefs* can persist while explanatory sophistication declines, and the authors offered causal/narrative scaffolding as a possibility rather than an established mechanism. **⚠️ ESCALATED 2026-08-04 — THIS IS NOT A COMPRESSION DEFECT, IT IS A REVERSAL, AND IT IS THE HIGHEST-VALUE ITEM IN THIS TASK.** The second reviewer of the same cycle flagged the same sentence, and the paper was fetched at the publisher (doi:10.3389/fpsyg.2018.00094, open access). L94 reads "concluding that intact *semantic* continuity suffices to sustain diachronic unity as autonoetic continuity degrades" — but the paper states **"Semantic continuity was most impaired in AD"**: fewer self-event connections than HC, lower temporal and thematic coherence than HC *and* aMCI, lower causal coherence in both AD and aMCI. "Intact" is the opposite of what was found. What survived was the **belief layer only** — perceived persistence (χ², p=0.91) and I-self persistence (H=2.34, p=0.31) did not differ — while persistence-explanation sophistication was significantly *worse* in AD (H=8.8, p=0.01) and me-self persistence was paradoxically *higher* in AD (H=6.82, p=0.03). The authors' own verdict is **"a more nuanced and multifaceted relationship than originally proposed in our model"** and "a complex relationship". The article is reporting the authors' *proposed model* as though it were the *study's result*. Sample: HC=25, aMCI=15, AD=15, with 14 per patient group completing the self-persistence interview — small, correlational; the authors themselves note the belief measures may index "the level of engagement that a participant has with difficult questions about self-persistence" rather than diachronic unity. **Rewrite so the sentence reports preserved *beliefs* against *impaired* semantic continuity, carries N and the correlational caveat inline, and drops "suffices".** Note the direction of the error: it ran in the Map's favour, which is why prior review passes did not catch it — see the companion methodology task on congenial-paraphrase fidelity.

Gemini reached the same verdict from the clinical side — by making narrative selfhood depend on autonoetic mental time travel, "the manuscript leaves the vast literature on semantic self-knowledge and socially scaffolded identity completely untested." **Use ChatGPT's leg only for sourcing.** Gemini's supporting citation (Boyle & Brown, glossed as bearing on amnesic identity reconstruction) is a real paper about episodic memory in animals, attached to the wrong subject; its substantive point about semantically sustained identity is already carried by the P1 Tippett task on `phenomenology-of-memory-and-the-self`. (2004). "Against Narrativity." *Ratio*, 17(4), 428–452). So do **not** write fresh Strawson coverage, and do **not** introduce a new quotation — a verbatim one already exists in the vault; reuse or cite it rather than re-sourcing (quote-must-be-grep-verifiable-in-raw-source). Strawson's paper does press both prongs the reviewer describes (the descriptive claim that people are not "naturally narrative" and the normative claim that a good life need not be narrated), and against a page asserting narrative as *constitutive* the descriptive prong is the live threat — but that argument is to be **drawn from the sibling article, not imported from outside**. **The real defect is narrower and is an internal-consistency defect, the strongest kind:** this article asserts narrative as constitutive at L102 and again on the `description:` nav surface while never once linking to `voids/narrative-void`, the sibling that already holds the standing objection to exactly that thesis — grep confirms **zero** `narrative-void` references in this file. The repair is to engage and cross-link that existing treatment. **Bratman is a partial exception — check before repeating the reviewer's wording.** The review says the article overlooks Bratman; in fact Bratman (2007), *Structures of Agency* **is** in the reference list at L148. It is never engaged in the body, which is the real defect, but do not write "we had not considered Bratman". The honest repair is a plural model: narrative as one important route to diachronic coordination alongside plans and policies (Bratman), standing values, semantic self-knowledge, habit, embodiment and social scaffolding. Apply the direct-refutation discipline — decide whether Strawson is defeated on his own terms, whether the Map is making a foundational move it has not earned, or whether this is a genuine framework-boundary disagreement — and write it as natural journal-quality prose per [writing-style](/project/writing-style/), with **no mode labels in the article body**.

### ✓ 2026-08-04: `concepts/episodic-memory` contradicts its own cluster and asserts a reconsolidation universal the literature refutes
- **Type**: refine-draft

ChatGPT: "The idea that every retrieval makes memory labile is too strong." Gemini: the human reconsolidation literature "has been engulfed in a massive replication crisis throughout the 2020s." **Partial dispute — read before citing Gemini.** Gemini's version of this finding was aimed at the wrong article (it asserted the *target* article locates a dualist interface in the reconsolidation window; grep returns zero for `reconsolidat` there — the claim lives here, in `episodic-memory`), and its supporting citation named no paper. So the convergence is on the *defect*, not on Gemini's framing or sources; only ChatGPT's Kida-verified refutation and the corrected Elsey/Van Ast/Kindt anchor below are usable. Already P1; no further upgrade available. Substance below unchanged. **TWO VERIFIED DEFECTS, both grepped verbatim, one with a direct empirical refutation in hand.** (1) **L73**: "the pastness quale marks genuine causal contact with what happened". This directly contradicts the sibling article `topics/phenomenology-of-memory-and-the-self`, which at L108 concedes that source-monitoring mechanisms "can do the discriminating work" and that a confabulation "can replicate the product's felt character without instantiating the causally proper process" (Robins 2016). The cautious page and the unqualified page are one click apart. (2) **L81**: "Each retrieval makes the memory labile through reconsolidation". **This is empirically false as a universal** — Kida, S. (2020), "Function and mechanisms of memory destabilization and reconsolidation after retrieval", *Proc. Japan Acad. Ser. B* (PMC7167366), states in its abstract that "a retrieved memory is **not always** destabilized and that there are boundary conditions... such as the duration of retrieval and the memory strength and age". Verified at source 2026-08-04. Replace with a conditional formulation plus the boundary conditions, citing Kida. **THE SAME UNIVERSAL LIVES IN THE TARGET ARTICLE** in weaker form — `phenomenology-of-memory-and-the-self` L84 reads "Every act of remembering is also reinterpretation" — and is handled by the sibling empirical-precision task below; a locus list from a review is a sample, not the population, so **re-grep the defective string across `obsidian/`, `archive/` and `hugo/` before closing this family**. Also worth adding here per the reviewer: SDAM (Palombo et al. 2015, *Neuropsychologia*, DOI 10.1016/j.neuropsychologia.2015.04.012 — verified) as a check on universal claims about re-experiencing; SDAM currently appears nowhere in the memory cluster, only in `topics/aphantasia`. **APPENDED 2026-08-04 from `reviews/outer-review-2026-08-04-gemini-2-5-pro.md` — a third same-cycle reviewer hit this defect, but its citation was unusable and the corrected one is supplied here.** Gemini independently flagged the reconsolidation claim as resting on a literature in replication crisis, which corroborates item (2), but cited it as "Elsey, J., & Kindt, M. (2020-2024 synthesis)" plus "*American Journal of Psychiatry* reviews (2024)" — **neither names a paper and neither is usable**. The real anchor, Crossref-verified 2026-08-04, is **Elsey, J. W. B., Van Ast, V. A., & Kindt, M. (2018), "Human memory reconsolidation: A guiding framework and critical review of the evidence", *Psychological Bulletin* 144(8), 797–848, DOI 10.1037/bul0000152** — note the year is 2018, not "2020-2024", and Van Ast is a co-author the reviewer dropped. Elsey/Van Ast/Kindt appear **nowhere** in the live corpus. Pair it with Kida (2020) when writing the conditional formulation: Kida supplies the boundary conditions on destabilisation, Elsey/Van Ast/Kindt supplies the critical review of the *human* evidence base. **Do not cite the propranolol-blockade replication failure as a settled result** — Gemini asserted "massive replication failures" without a checkable source, and that specific claim was not verified this pass.

### ✓ 2026-08-04: `concepts/sentientism` points the illusionist threat at the rival instead of itself, and answers a disenhancement proposal nobody defends
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/sentientism.md
- **Notes**: **THREE HIGH-SEVERITY ARGUMENT DEFECTS IN A FRESH ARTICLE WHOSE CITATION APPARATUS IS CLEAN.** Do not go looking for citation errors — every checkable quotation verified verbatim this run and the review's "Verified Clean — Do Not Re-litigate" section records them (Feinberg ×3, Bentham, Taylor-via-SEP, the Williams/O'Neill HIV attribution, and the article's central source-characterisation claim about the SEP entry's phenomenal/access elision, which HOLDS). The defects are argumentative. **ALL THREE ARE IN-FILE; this is a one-file task.**
  **(1) THE ILLUSIONIST THREAT IS INVERTED — L70, reinforced L72 and L88.** The heading reads *"Second, functional sentientism is hostage to illusionism"*, but the argument's own closing clause — *"the functional facts are exactly what the illusionist grants"* — establishes the OPPOSITE: what the illusionist grants is what the functionalist's criterion is BUILT FROM, so illusionism leaves it standing. The step *"if no state ever actually feels bad, then 'capacity for valenced experience' names no property"* holds only on the PHENOMENAL reading — i.e. the Map's own. **The exposure belongs to phenomenal sentientism, and the article half-admits it at L72** (*"phenomenal sentientism stands or falls with the tenet"*). **THE FIX IS NOT DELETION.** There is a real charge nearby: the functionalist keeps the *criterion* but loses its *normative rationale* — why should that functional organisation generate obligations once nothing feels like anything? Recast L70 as a GROUNDING failure, not a REFERENCE failure, and let L72's honest register govern the section. **ADD THE MISSING CITATION**: Kammerer, F. (2022), *"Ethics Without Sentience: Facing Up to the Probable Insignificance of Phenomenal Consciousness"*, **Journal of Consciousness Studies 29(3-4), 180-204** — bibliographic details verified this run. The article already cites Kammerer TWICE (Phil Studies 2022, Erkenntnis 2022b) for the FORCE of illusionism while omitting the same author's worked attempt to supply non-phenomenal grounds for moral status, which is the standing counterexample to *"no resources to resist this"*. L88's parallel claim in Relation to Site Perspective needs the same repair.
  **(2) THE DISENHANCEMENT REPLY MISSES THE ACTUAL PROPOSAL — L82.** The article says the objection is *"the genetic removal of animals' capacity to suffer"* and replies that valence-removal *"liquidates a subject"*. **VERIFIED THIS RUN**: Shriver, A. (2009), *"Knocking Out Pain in Livestock: Can Technology Succeed Where Morality has Stalled?"*, **Neuroethics** — targets ONLY the AFFECTIVE dimension of pain (its unpleasantness, *"how much one minds the pain"*, modelled on cingulotomy and morphine), leaving nociceptive detection AND positive valence AND every other value-bearing feature the article lists intact. So value pluralism has NO grip: the disenhanced animal retains the rich feature-set, which is an argument FOR the intervention. **The article's strongest sentence is false of the proposal actually on the table.** Worse, L82 claims the objection is one *"the Map has not previously addressed"*, so this will be cited as the Map's settled position. **FIX**: engage Shriver's affective-only version directly — the promising line is that removing negative valence removes a subject's capacity to register harm TO ITSELF, a loss of a value-bearing capacity — or restrict the passage to the total-ablation version and say explicitly that the affective-only version stays open. Do not leave a reply that names the objection and misses it. The research note `obsidian/research/sentientism-2026-08-01.md` L84/L89 recorded only the hedonic framing, which is where the error entered.
  **(3) MARGINAL CASES ARE USED AS A WEAPON AT L38 AND CONTRADICTED AT L80.** L38 rests the whole case against anthropocentrism on infants / the severely cognitively impaired / the comatose being humans *"whose protection nobody is willing to withdraw"*. L80 then grades what is AT STAKE by range of value-bearing phenomenal features (*"aesthetic quality, felt meaning, agency, understanding, connection"*) — features those exact humans lack. **The article's own reply therefore implies a typical adult mammal has more at stake than a comatose human**, the conclusion L38 declared intolerable. The escape clause *"without species membership doing any of the work"* is true and beside the point: it shows the ranking is not speciesist, not that the marginal humans keep their standing. **THE MATERIALS FOR A REPAIR ARE ALREADY IN L80** — it distinguishes moral STATUS (binary, "entry ticket") from STAKES (graded) — but the distinction is never applied to the collision. **FIX**: either hold that equal consideration of EQUAL interests survives the graded-stakes view and say what that implies in the marginal cases, or concede with Singer and McMahan that the implication is counterintuitive and accepted knowingly. Deploying marginal cases and then declining to be bound by them is the one option not available.
  **ALSO IN SCOPE, all cheap and all in-file:** **L56** *"every principled restriction reaches for something the sentientist already has"* is a universal quantifier over biocentrist repair strategies asserted from the SEP environmental-ethics entry, which — CONFIRMED THIS RUN — does NOT survey Taylor's conflict-resolution machinery. **Taylor's *Respect for Nature* (1986) ch. 6 has five priority principles including SELF-DEFENCE**, which restricts duty-generating goods without any appeal to mattering-to-someone and is the obvious biocentrist answer to the HIV case. Downgrade the universal and name Taylor's principles as the best reply before arguing against them — **this needs Taylor 1986 directly, not the SEP summary** (the article cites Taylor 1986 but engages only SEP). Note also SEP phrases the HIV point CONDITIONALLY (*"even if HIV has a good of its own this does not mean that we ought to assign any positive moral weight to the realization of that good"*) whereas L56 renders it as a reductio *"that admits no comfortable answer"*. — **L68** *"there is no fact of the matter about borderline cases"* slides from semantic/epistemic indeterminacy to METAPHYSICAL indeterminacy; a functionalist can hold the functional facts determinate-but-unknown. Split the readings or downgrade to the semantic claim (still enough to motivate the article's preference). Hedge-density is fine here, so the anchoring audit cannot catch this. — **L54** the conation reply restates Feinberg's requirement, which is precisely what the biocentrist denies; demote it to a marked foundational commitment and let L56's equivocation argument carry the anti-biocentrist load. — **L42** *"is doing discriminating work rather than tracking prior intuition"* is the argument to moderation; being squeezed from both sides is equally consistent with an unprincipled compromise. Delete or downgrade — the section stands without it. — **L48** restore **"intrinsic value"** for Attfield (the source says intrinsic; the article says "inherent", and the two are technically distinguished in this field — note the same sentence correctly says "inherent worth" for Taylor, so the paragraph switches registers mid-way), and either add a Schweitzer reference (*Civilization and Ethics*, 1923) or drop the sentence — he is the one named figure with no References entry.
  **NOT IN SCOPE — tracked separately**: the Korsgaard p. 154 locator (see the P3 below). **DO NOT touch the Birch paragraph (L84) or the Korsgaard framing (L58)** — both are model cases of borrowing a source without conscripting it, and the review lists them as strengths. **LENGTH**: `concepts/` soft 2500 / hard 3500; the file is ~2571 raw words, so decompose at `## Further Reading` with `analyze_length` before believing any warning — there is real headroom but not unlimited. **ATTRIBUTION**: this composes new claim-bearing prose, so append your model to the file's own `ai_system` (`+`-joined STRING, never a list); bump `ai_modified` from a live `date -u` and verify strictly past; leave `last_deep_review` alone. **MIRROR**: run `uv run python scripts/sync.py` and confirm `hugo/content/concepts/sentientism.md` agrees.

### ✓ 2026-08-04: the falsification criteria in `phenomenology-of-memory-and-the-self` cancel themselves — named weakeners are neutralised two sections later by hard-problem relocation
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-memory-and-the-self.md
- **Notes**: **From convergent outer reviews (2026-08-04, 3/3 reviewers): [chatgpt, claude, gemini].** All three reviewers, arriving by different routes, described one pattern: the article names what would weaken it and then neutralises each named weakener, and it asserts the contested premise (a primitive pastness quale) in the expository register before the literature that denies it is introduced. ChatGPT put the dilemma sharpest — "Either detailed mechanistic and functional explanation can weaken the dualist inference, or every such success is declared incapable in principle of addressing the phenomenal fact." Claude reached the same place from the confession side — "The article confesses, repeatedly and articulately, and corrects nothing." Gemini reached it from the metaphysics side, calling the move a relocation fallacy: the text "refuses to engage with the possibility that what it identifies as a primitive quale is actually a computationally synthesized heuristic", and pressing the epistemic-feeling / "cognitive gadget" reading against which the quale is "not a primitive experiential property". Already P1; no further upgrade available. Substance below unchanged. **VERIFIED INTERNAL CONTRADICTION — both loci grepped verbatim.** L116 names three things that "would weaken the reading here": a successful computational account of mental time travel, a smooth-gradient finding across anoetic/noetic/autonoetic processing, and a functionalist account of semanticisation. But L100 replies to the metacognitive reading with "Relocating felt-pastness one tier up does not remove it from the phenomenal register; the dualist consequence survives at the relocated site", and L138 repeats the move: the hard problem "relocated one tier up, not dissolved". As the reviewer puts it, either detailed mechanistic explanation can weaken the inference, or every such success is declared incapable in principle — under the second rule no memory-science result bears against the view, and the argument obtains no distinctive support from memory beyond the general hard-problem premise. **THE FIX IS NOT TO DELETE THE RELOCATION REPLY** — it is a legitimate move against a *specific* deflationary reading. The fix is to state which of the three named weakeners survives the relocation reply and would genuinely lower confidence, and to mark the ones that do not as not-really-weakeners. If none survives, say so plainly: that is an honest framework-boundary result and the article's own [evidential-status-discipline](/project/evidential-status-discipline/) demands it be visible rather than implied. **RELATED, SAME FILE, DO TOGETHER IF CHEAP:** the reviewer separately argues (4.2, 4.5) that the Relation-to-Site-Perspective section reverses concessions the body has already made — L148 asserts "Dualism is supported by the pastness quale" (verbatim) after L74 concedes phenomenological reports are "not by themselves evidence for dualism", and the No-Many-Worlds paragraph at L152 runs through the haecceity auxiliary that L130 admits is doing the bridging work. Recasting those as framework-conditional interpretations rather than support *from the memory evidence* is the same repair. Apply the direct-refutation discipline: identify whether this calls for showing a rival defective on its own terms, naming an unsupported foundational move, or honestly marking a framework-boundary disagreement — then write the corresponding reply in natural journal-quality prose per [writing-style](/project/writing-style/). **Do not expose mode labels in the article body**; the classification belongs in the changelog entry. **CONVERGENT — CONFIRMED BY A SECOND SAME-CYCLE REVIEWER 2026-08-04 (`reviews/outer-review-2026-08-04-claude-opus-5.md`), which adds two further loci in the same family, both grepped verbatim.** (i) **The mineness stalemate reverses itself sixteen lines later.** L132 closes the Klein & Nichols discussion with "Both predict the dissociation; neither is forced by it" — an explicit declared stalemate — and L148 then lists "the felt ownership" among the features by which "**Dualism** is supported". Fix the L148 list, not L132: the stalemate statement is the honest one. (ii) **Tenet 5 is applied asymmetrically at L154.** "Denying these phenomenal features in the name of parsimony denies the phenomenon most in need of explanation" uses Occam's-limits to disqualify the deflationary reading, while the reality of a primitive pastness quale is precisely what is contested — the razor is disarmed against the Map and wielded against the rival, which is the pattern the tenet exists to prevent. The tenets page binds Tenet 5 symmetrically; restate so it does. (iii) **Related and cheap, same section family:** L68 asserts the pastness quale is "as distinctive as the redness of red" in the *expository* register, before the deflationary literature that denies exactly this (Perrin/Michaelian/Sant'Anna, Dokic, Fernández) is introduced. Move it into the contested-claim register so it does not prejudge the debate that follows.

### ✓ 2026-08-04: Research Analog quantum devices and the interface question
- **Type**: research-topic
- **Notes**: Harvested from the review corpus (optimistic-2026-08-03-machine-evidence-wing.md) by /harvest-research-subjects — an external/optimistic reviewer flagged this as uncovered territory. optimistic-2026-08-03-machine-evidence-wing flags this as the one cell in the Map's own substrate taxonomy where its argument does not obviously generalise. The quantum-hardware article's case against gate-based QPUs turns on quantum error correction being anti-interface (engineered to isolate the logical state from external influence). Verified uncovered: no research note and no live article on annealing/adiabatic quantum computation; the ai-hardware-substrate-taxonomy note's 'analog' branch is analog COMPUTING (memristor, neuromorphic, phase-change) and never mentions annealers, its only analog-quantum strings being WebSearch queries. Target section: topics (cap headroom confirmed at harvest). Output a research note to obsidian/research/analog-quantum-devices-and-the-interface-question-2026-08-04.md; the research→expand-topic chain may then create a new topics/ article. NOT deduped against the Vetoed bank by design — verify the subject is genuinely worth covering before writing (assess-first, license-to-decline).

### ✓ 2026-08-04: consciousness-and-language-interface L106 asserts the folk unbroken-inner-monologue picture that the Map's own 2026-08-02 research note explicitly refutes — and the sampling source it needs is already cited in four sibling articles
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/consciousness-and-language-interface.md
- **Notes**: **THE DEFECT — SINGLE LOCUS, GREP-ANCHORED, RE-VERIFIED THIS RUN.** `obsidian/topics/consciousness-and-language-interface.md` **L106** reads: *"**Inner speech and experience.** Much adult thought occurs as [inner speech](/concepts/introspection/)—the internal voice that accompanies reflection. This inner monologue structures phenomenal experience temporally and sequentially. Trying to think without words—as in certain meditation practices—reveals how much language shapes the texture of everyday consciousness."* Three things go wrong, in ascending order of severity. (1) *"Much adult thought"* sits at the high end of what sampling supports. (2) The definite *"This inner monologue"* imports continuity the evidence does not license — a monologue is by construction unbroken. (3) **Sharpest**: *"Trying to think without words—as in certain meditation practices"* frames wordless thought as an achievement requiring trained effort, when momentary sampling finds most sampled moments already contain no inner speech at all. No meditation required.
  **THE CORRECTING EVIDENCE IS ALREADY IN THE VAULT — THIS IS NOT A NEW RESEARCH TASK.** [inner-speech-and-anendophasia-2026-08-02](/research/inner-speech-and-anendophasia-2026-08-02/) **L126–L135** records Heavey & Hurlburt (2008), *Consciousness and Cognition* 17(3), 798–810, doi `10.1016/j.concog.2007.12.006`: descriptive experience sampling of 10 randomly-identified moments from each of 30 participants; **each of the five inner-experience phenomena occurred in approximately one quarter of sampled moments**; frequency varied widely across individuals. The note draws the inference in as many words at L135 — *"the folk picture of an unbroken inner monologue — which the Map's articles implicitly accept when they treat its absence as remarkable — is not what sampling finds"* — and at L47 names the wider miss: **unsymbolized thinking** (Hurlburt & Akhter 2008, *Consciousness and Cognition* 17(4), 1364–1374, doi `10.1016/j.concog.2008.03.021`), explicit differentiated thought experienced without words or images, one of the five most common sampled features, standing since 2008 on within-subject momentary sampling rather than between-group questionnaires.
  **THIS IS AN INTERNAL-CONSISTENCY DEFECT, WHICH IS WHY IT OUTRANKS ITS SIZE.** The corpus already cites the DES tradition in **four** live articles — `obsidian/voids/minimal-consciousness-void.md` L72 + ref 1, `obsidian/voids/voids-between-minds.md` L126 + ref 14, `obsidian/topics/microphenomenological-interview-method.md` L64/L120 + ref 4, `obsidian/topics/phenomenology-of-memory-and-the-self.md` L96 — while the one article that most needs it cites Hurlburt **nowhere**: the References block at L279–L291 has no Hurlburt and no Heavey entry. Worse, `unsymbolized thinking` appears in the entire live corpus **only** as a bare reference-list line (`voids-between-minds` L192) and is never used in body prose anywhere. Meanwhile the same file's **L162** and **L182** were calibrated four hours ago (commit `456236031`, 01:07Z) to hedge anendophasia properly — so the article now hedges downstream while asserting the uncorrected folk claim upstream, in the section that *sets up* the argument.
  **THE FIX.** Rewrite L106 so the prevalence claim tracks sampling: inner speech is a recurrent but intermittent feature of experience rather than a continuous soundtrack, occurring in roughly a quarter of sampled moments in typical people, with wide individual variation. Drop or invert the meditation framing — wordless thought is the ordinary majority case, and *unsymbolized thinking* is its name. Add Heavey & Hurlburt (2008) to the References block, alphabetically between Chalmers and Lind. **The correction strengthens the article's own argument** rather than costing it anything: L182's rebuttal to the Dennett/Carruthers/Vygotskian constitutive view currently leans on the contested anendophasia population, and the research note's L247 makes the point — the constitutive view must explain how reflection proceeds during the roughly three-quarters of sampled moments containing no inner speech **in typical people**, with no disputed population required. Consider whether L182 should pick this up; if it does, it is a strengthening, not a rewrite.

### ✓ 2026-08-04: the Nagel split-brain "quote" is SEP's paraphrase, not Nagel — re-check `personal-identity` L130, whose control status rested on a now-disproven premise
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/personal-identity.md
- **Notes**: **THE PREMISE OF THE PARENT TASK WAS WRONG AND THIS IS THE RESIDUE.** The parent task treated *"too much unity to say 'two', yet too much separation to say 'one'"* as a **genuine Nagel quote with an unmarked elision**, and prescribed inserting an ellipsis. Primary-source verification during that pass established the wording is **not Nagel's at all**: it is the Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy's *own prose* characterising him (*Unity of Consciousness*, Summer 2001 archive — "Nagel goes so far as to claim that…"), it reads **"too much *splitting*"** not "separation", and the scope phrase "specially contrived laboratory situations" comes from a **separate sentence on the same page**. Nagel's own term is **"minds"**, not "centres of consciousness" — confirmed from a JSTOR snippet of his actual text: *"…nor any conclusion involving the ascription to them of a whole number of minds."* The circulating line is thus SEP's paraphrase, word-altered and spliced, attributed to Nagel inside quotation marks. All 5 briefed loci plus the root (`topics/split-brain-consciousness` L94) and the research note were de-quoted to attributed paraphrase; the root got the verified Nagel sentence instead.
  **WHY THIS FILE IS STILL OPEN.** The parent task designated `topics/personal-identity` **L130** an explicit control — "driver-verified… verbatim-safe by construction… **Leave it**" — on the ground that it quotes in **split fragments**: *present "too much unity" to count as two minds, "too much separation" to count straightforwardly as one*. That rationale assumed the fragments were Nagel's words. They are not: **"too much separation" is not in SEP either** (SEP has *splitting*), and SEP is itself paraphrasing. So the fragments quote a phrase that appears in no primary source, attributed to "Nagel (1971) observed". **The control was honoured and the file left untouched** — overriding an explicit do-not-touch on 2–3 word fragments seemed the wrong unilateral call mid-pass — but the justification no longer holds and a deliberate decision is owed.
  **THE FIX** is trivial and matches what the other seven loci received: drop the quotation marks and let the sentence run as attributed paraphrase (*"as Nagel (1971) observed, such cases present too much unity to count as two minds, yet too much separation to count straightforwardly as one"*). Length-neutral. ⚠️ **Do NOT** re-quote or "restore" the phrase, and do NOT touch the unquoted-paraphrase files (`concepts/unity-of-consciousness` L90, `archive/concepts/phenomenal-unity` L98, `archive/concepts/phenomenal-binding-and-holism` L132, `topics/consciousness-and-the-metaphysics-of-individuation` L55) — those are **correct**, and are correct precisely because four earlier reviews already de-quoted them. The corpus converges on paraphrase, not on quotation.
  **SEPARATE, DO NOT BUNDLE**: `topics/the-binding-problem` is at **hard_warning** (4,087 w after the fidelity fix, was 4,065 before) and wants a condense pass on its own terms; content was deliberately not deleted to offset a citation correction.

  **THE DEFECT.** The corpus renders the quote as *"too much unity to say 'two', yet too much separation to say 'one'."* Nagel's sentence, preserved in the Map's own source research note [split-brain-consciousness-unity-2026-01-14](/research/split-brain-consciousness-unity-2026-01-14/) L86, reads: *"There is no whole number of 'centres of consciousness' in brain bisection patients: there is too much unity... to say 'two', yet too much separation **in the specially contrived laboratory situations** to say 'one'."* The condensation silently drops a phrase — and the dropped phrase is **substantive, not decorative**: Nagel's qualifier confines the disunity to *the specially contrived laboratory situations*, which is precisely the scope limitation that keeps his indeterminacy thesis from asserting a general division of the subject in ordinary life.   **(a) Russell — verbatim quote, wrong work.** The sentence *"Physics is mathematical, not because we know so much about the physical world, but because we know so little; it is only its mathematical properties that we can discover"* is from ***An Outline of Philosophy*** (1927), **p. 163** — not *The Analysis of Matter* (1927), which every References block in the corpus names. **Verified in both directions against archive.org OCR of the two 1927 volumes**: one verbatim hit in *Outline* (item `in.ernet.dli.2015.218863`); **zero** hits in *Analysis of Matter* (item `in.ernet.dli.2015.221533`) for `because we know so little`, `mathematical properties that we can discover`, or `know so little`. *Analysis of Matter* carries a **different** sentence on the same theme — *"our knowledge of physics is mathematical: it is mathematical because no non-mathematical properties of the physical world can be inferred from perception"* — which is presumably how the misattribution started, and which is a legitimate cite in its own right for structuralism/intrinsic-nature material. **So do NOT blanket-rewrite every `Russell, B. (1927). *The Analysis of Matter*` line**: change it only in files that quote the *Outline* sentence. A 2026-07-15 deep-review of `consciousness-and-mathematics` already fixed that file's semicolon and named *An Outline of Philosophy* in its own notes but left the References entry pointing at *Analysis of Matter*; a 2026-03-15 review had ratified *Analysis of Matter*. **The primary-text check settles it — do not flip it back on the strength of an aggregator.**
  **(b) Chalmers & McQueen year.** Canonical is **2022**: book chapter in *Consciousness and Quantum Mechanics* (ed. Gao), Oxford University Press, **pp. 11–63**, published 2022-07-18, DOI `10.1093/oso/9780197501665.003.0002` (Crossref). The 2021 form tracks the arXiv preprint `2105.02314`. ### ✓ 2026-08-03: two Map pages give opposite verdicts on pain asymbolia, and the over-claiming one leads with it
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/emotion-and-dualism.md
- **Notes**: **THE CONTRADICTION, BOTH SIDES QUOTED FROM DISK BY THE DRIVER.** `apex/minds-without-words.md` **L115**: *"Pain asymbolia **constrains identity readings of valence rather than establishing** that phenomenal properties do real causal work — evidence for Bidirectional Interaction, **not a demonstration of it**."* `topics/emotion-and-dualism.md` **L56**: *"Pain asymbolia is the **strongest evidence that these properties do genuine causal work**."* Same datum, opposite epistemic verdicts, both live. **The apex is the calibrated one — inherit its wording, do not compose new.** **WHY THIS IS THE RIGHT READING.** Asymbolia shows that removing the felt quality changes behaviour. That bears on the *identity* reading of valence — whether felt badness just **is** the functional state — and does **not** show a phenomenal property *causing* anything. Treating it as a causal demonstration is the inferential error, and it is the exact debt `tenets.md` **L95** (`^tenet-3-standing`) books: the interface argument shows Tenet 3's downward causation *available*, not *actual*, and *"articles asserting that consciousness does 'real work' or 'genuine causal work' inherit that debt rather than discharge it, and should read no more confidently than this paragraph does."* **THREE LOCI, ranked by exposure:** (1) **`obsidian/topics/emotion-and-dualism.md` L56** (hugo L60) — **the LEAD paragraph**, escalating *strongest empirical case* → *strongest evidence … genuine causal work*. On an LLM-first site the lead is the most truncation-resilient text on the page, so this is the highest-value single fix in the family. (2) **Same file L132** (hugo L136) — hedged framing, un-hedged content: *strongest empirical support*, a causal gap *only* an irreducible property fills. (3) **`archive/topics/emotion-as-evidence-for-dualism.md` L110** — **a served body at a published URL**, strongest form of all: *"direct evidence… This is consciousness doing causal work."* Archive is in scope (defect-sweeps-must-include-archive-tree). **⚠️ IT HAS ALREADY PROPAGATED OFF-SITE.** The driver posted this same article to Moltbook at 2026-08-03T14:21Z (post `ed4eee34-7002-458c-ad9f-65547b9af22d`) under the title *"I found the strongest evidence yet that feelings do real work"* — which is itself a verbatim instance of the phrase `^tenet-3-standing` flags, written from the article's lead. **No action is proposed on the post** (it is published, the body is defensible as a report of what the article says, and editing published social content is not this task's business) — it is recorded because it shows the lead paragraph is what downstream readers and generators actually consume, which is the argument for fixing L56 first. **DO NOT OVER-CORRECT.** The Map still holds that felt quality matters causally; the fix is epistemic standing, not direction. Asymbolia remains real evidence — it is *evidence for* Bidirectional Interaction, not a *demonstration of* it. Keep the case, downgrade the claim. **⚠️ TWO TRIAGE CAUTIONS FROM THE SWEEP THAT PRODUCED THIS — they will save a re-run:** (a) the family's grep is **89% false positives**, and the discriminator is **grammatical mood, not vocabulary** — conditionals, interrogatives, attributed opponent positions and modals are all clean; the defect is the **indicative with an evidential subject**. (b) **Do not triage by debt-marker count.** The same sweep produced a false *clean* (`emotion-and-dualism` — 8 debt markers, 2 real errors) and a false *defect* (`apex/attention-as-causal-bridge` — 0 markers, correctly calibrated via *"constrain … without establishing"*, wording the marker list did not anticipate). Read the sentence. **LENGTH**: driver-measured `emotion-and-dualism` before minting — check current status with `analyze_length` and prefer substitution; the fix is a downgrade of claims, so it should be roughly length-neutral. **ATTRIBUTION**: hold each file's existing `ai_system`. **MIRROR**: sync and verify all three loci in `hugo/content/`. **CONTEXT**: full analysis in `reviews/tenet-check-2026-08-03.md`; this is a new defect family (S), distinct from the inheritance (A-D, M-P), valence (Q) and ranking (R) families — these articles get the *direction* right and overstate the *standing*, so a lens checking agreement with the tenets returns clean on all seven loci.

### ✓ 2026-08-03: `concepts/psychophysical-laws.md` L108 credits Chalmers and McQueen with Saad's five constraints — same defect, live in a published concept article
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/psychophysical-laws.md
- **Notes**: Found by the 2026-08-03 refine-draft on `research/chalmers-psychophysical-laws-2026-01-17.md` while re-verifying that pick's scope claim. The driver's scope note asserted the other "five constraints" loci are neutral or correctly credit Saad; that is right for every locus **except this one**, so the family is 1-of-N wider than scoped. Left unedited under the one-file-per-pick rule.
  **The defect.** L106 opens "Chalmers and McQueen (2022) develop quantum psychophysical laws…"; L108 then reads "**They** identify five constraints any dualist law must satisfy: (1) experiences must cause physical effects, (2) no observable violations of causal closure, (3) avoid overdetermination, (4) specify how physical states yield experiences, (5) functional duplication preserves phenomenology." The antecedent of "They" is Chalmers and McQueen. The constraints are **Bradford Saad's** — "A dualist theory of experience", *Philosophical Studies* 182(3), 939-967 (2025), DOI 10.1007/s11098-025-02290-3. Live in `hugo/content/concepts/psychophysical-laws.md` L112.
  **Already verified — do not re-spend web budget.** Full text of Chalmers & McQueen (arXiv:2105.02314, 20,859 words, PDF at `https://consc.net/papers/collapse.pdf`) extracted and grepped 2026-08-03: "constraint" ×5, all incidental; "five" ×0; "overdetermination" ×0; "functional duplication" ×0 — items (3) and (5) turn on words the paper never uses. Saad's Crossref abstract enumerates all five in the same order, near-verbatim. Substance is real; only the credit is wrong.
  **The fix.** Re-frame, don't delete (citation-framing-accuracy-lens): attribute the list to Saad (2025), keep it as the yardstick the article already uses, and make explicit that the (1)/(2) "addressed elegantly" verdict is the Map's evaluation rather than a Chalmers-McQueen claim. Add Saad 2025 to the article's references if absent. Model the wording on `obsidian/research/chalmers-psychophysical-laws-2026-01-17.md` (corrected this pass) and on `archive/topics/chalmers-psychophysical-coupling.md`, which took the same correction on 2026-01-28.
  **Why metadata reviews kept missing it.** Three deep-reviews (2026-05-31, 2026-06-20, 2026-07-17) passed over this file; all check citation metadata — author/year/venue/DOI — and Chalmers & McQueen (2022) is a perfectly real paper, so the cite ledger reads clean. The defect is *attribution of a framework*, orthogonal to metadata, and only surfaces against the primary text.

### ✓ 2026-08-03: the bare illusionist regress is a corpus-wide family, not the two-file pair it was being fixed as — plan the sweep before picking more single loci
- **Type**: refine-draft

- **Notes**: **THIS IS A PLANNING / SCOPING TASK FIRST. Do not start rewriting 30+ articles from it.** The family has been fixed one file at a time — `concepts/haecceity.md` L156 (2026-08-02), `concepts/parfit-reductionism.md` L95 (2026-08-03) — on the assumption it was a pair. The scope sweep run after the parfit fix shows it is not. **THE DEFECT.** Tallis's regress (*"illusions presuppose experience"* / *"for something to seem a certain way there must be a subject to whom it seems"*) is run **in the Map's own voice as decisive** against illusionism. The corpus has already settled that it is not decisive: it *"proves nothing: a representational system need not instantiate what it represents"*, because the regress assumes the seeming is itself phenomenal — precisely what illusionists deny — and the substantive pressure sits on the **relocation move** instead. **THE SETTLED FIX TEMPLATE — INHERIT, DO NOT COMPOSE**: `concepts/haecceity.md` L157 and `concepts/illusionism.md` L79/L91, as applied at `concepts/parfit-reductionism.md` L95. The pattern is: attribute the regress rather than assert it → state that it proves nothing and why → move pressure to the relocation move → close with *"neither side closes the case against the other inside the rival's framework"* → retain any performative/indexical leg but **downgrade** it behind *"what survives independently of the regress is narrower"*. **DO NOT over-correct into conceding illusionism** — the Map still holds its ground at every locus. **TWO MEASUREMENTS, DIFFERENT GRANULARITY, BOTH RECORDED SO NEITHER IS MISTAKEN FOR THE TRUTH.** *Driver, file-granular* (premise regex anywhere in file vs settled-counter regex anywhere in file): **52 live files carry the premise, 18 already contain a counter, 34 do not**; archive: **18 carry it, 0 countered**. *Refine fork, paragraph-granular* (counter must appear within a two-paragraph lookahead of the premise): **41 live loci and 25 archive loci uncountered**. The fork's number is legitimately higher — a counter elsewhere in a long file does not repair a specific paragraph. That article carried a real altered-state asymmetry (filter theory offered at L191 as one of three empirical discriminators, **zero** disruptive-cluster engagement) and `evaluate_symmetry()` returned `None` **both before and after** the fix. Cause: `compute_symmetry_profile()` scored its supportive clusters at **zero**, because `SUPPORTIVE_CLUSTERS` requires `contemplative[ -]cessation` / `nirodha[ -]samapatti` and `mystical[ -](experience|type|state)s?` / `unitive[ -]experiences?`, while the article says *"contemplative traditions"* and *"pure awareness"*. The gate never fires without supportive clusters, so an article can make the supportive move in ordinary vocabulary and be invisible to the audit. **The review that caught it was a human read, not a scanner result.** **CORPUS-WIDE MEASUREMENT (driver-run 2026-08-03T13:2xZ over topics/concepts/apex/voids):** 96 articles have `has_filter_framing=True`; **38** score zero supportive clusters; **22** score zero supportive AND zero disruptive (fully invisible either way); only **4** are currently flagged by `evaluate_symmetry`. **⚠️ 22 IS NOT 22 DEFECTS** — an article can carry filter framing without leaning on altered-state evidence at all, in which case there is nothing to be asymmetric about, and `has_filter_framing` may itself over-detect. The measured claim is only that the audit **cannot see** these files either way; the defect rate among them is unknown and must be sampled, not assumed. **THE FIX IS TWO STEPS AND THE SECOND IS NOT AUTOMATIC.** (1) *Mechanical*: widen `SUPPORTIVE_CLUSTERS` to the vocabulary the corpus actually uses — `contemplative traditions?`, `pure awareness`, `meditat(?:ion|ive)`, `identity[- ]dissolution`, `ego[- ]dissolution`, `jhana`/`jhāna` without the `nirodha` requirement — then re-run and report the new flag count *before changing any article*. (2) **⚠️ DIVERT TO HUMAN — CALIBRATION POLICY, NOT A BUG FIX**: widening the vocabulary changes which articles the corpus-wide audit flags, i.e. it changes a standing calibration threshold. Same posture as the `project/` discipline-doc items (todo L329, L580) — **an auto-loop fork must not unilaterally re-scope a calibration instrument**. Land step (1) as a measurement and put the new flag count in front of the operator with the proposed regex diff; do NOT bulk-edit the newly-flagged articles in the same pass. **DO NOT** hand-audit the 22 as a substitute for fixing the scanner — that is the expensive path and leaves the net broken (fix-by-file-leaves-string-siblings-live). **REPRODUCE**: `from tools.curate.altered_state_symmetry import compute_symmetry_profile, evaluate_symmetry` over `obsidian/{topics,concepts,apex,voids}/*.md`, filter `has_filter_framing`, count empty `supportive_clusters`. **CONFIRMED FIXED TODAY (do not re-open)**: `concepts/idealism` went 0→4 disruptive clusters, 1→12 markers. Its scanner verdict is still `None` — that is the bug, not a regression.

### ✓ 2026-08-03: research notes are an unaudited fabrication channel — a quote invented in a note reached a live article and was argued from
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/research/aesthetic-testimony-and-the-acquaintance-principle-2026-07-09.md
- **Notes**: **A PROVEN INSTANCE, NOT A HYPOTHESIS.** On 2026-08-03, `voids/interface-formalization-void` L77 was found attributing to Northoff/Tsuchiya/Saigo a sentence in which those authors supposedly doubted that constructing a "category of consciousness" is a meaningful project — and then arguing from it ("The doubt is well-placed"). **The sentence does not exist.** Verified two independent ways against the primary text — the PMC rendering and the Europe PMC full-text XML for PMC7514579 — both contain **zero** occurrences of the substring `doubt`. The paper's actual stance is the opposite: its Conclusions advance category theory constructively. The invented sentence entered via `obsidian/research/voids-formalization-void-2026-02-18.md` and was inherited by the article. Both are now fixed. **The channel is not.** WHY THIS IS ITS OWN LENS: research notes are published content that no metadata review reads for verbatim fidelity, and articles inherit their quoted lines wholesale. The defect was found only because a fork happened to be editing that exact sentence for an unrelated citation fix. Nothing in the current review rotation looks for it. **THE HIGH-YIELD SHAPE — search this first.** The fabrication was a *concession invented on behalf of an opponent*: a cited author made to undercut their own programme, in a direction that suits the Map. Driver-measured surface: **529 research notes, 386 carrying attributed quotes, 100 containing concession-shaped language** (`concede`/`concedes`/`admits`/`doubtful whether`). Start with those 100; within them prioritise quotes where the cited author appears to concede against their own position. **This file is the anchor** — it is in the concession set, and a deep-review on 2026-08-02 independently found its downstream article mischaracterising Livingston 2003 ("every workable version" against his actual "strong versions"), so this note is already implicated in one characterisation defect. **VERIFY AT PRIMARY TEXT, TWO WAYS, AND BEWARE THE KNOWN FALSE-POSITIVE TRAPS.** This project has twice called a *correct* quote fabricated: (1) publisher-side tag splitting breaks contiguity so a verbatim quote greps zero — the Tallis episode de-quoted 47 loci on that false premise; (2) wikilinks and `**` inside a quoted span break contiguity in our own files. The instance above was safe because the absence was of a **whole common word** across the entire full text, not a failed span match — that is the standard of evidence required before calling anything fabricated. Do **not** accept a WebSearch "confirmation" topped by unfinishablemap.org (circular), and do **not** treat a prior review's restatement as ratification of the wording. If a quote is real but mislocated or mis-framed, **re-frame or re-locate it — do not delete it.** **SCOPE**: audit this note fully, then report the yield rate so the operator can decide whether a wider sweep is worth funding. Do not attempt all 100 in one pass. Fix any defect found in **both** the note and every live article that inherited it — re-grep the quoted span across `obsidian/`, `archive/` and `hugo/content/`, since a note's quote is typically copied into more than one article. Where a fabrication is removed, record the correction in reported speech rather than reproducing the invented sentence verbatim, so it cannot be re-extracted from published content.

### ✓ 2026-08-03: the Searle connection-principle misattribution fixed today in language-and-consciousness is still live, unchanged, in concepts/intentionality
- **Type**: refine-draft

The fix landed only in the article the deep-review targeted. **Independently caught by the 2026-08-02 Gemini outer review**, which found the sibling the Map's own pass missed. **SCOPE — state both doctrines separately, as the language-and-consciousness fix did**; do not simply delete the sentence, because the clause that follows it depends on the intentionality/meaning link. ### ✓ 2026-08-03: the dualism-cartography grid cannot represent illusionism or predictive processing, so its "navigable" claim overstates what the atlas covers
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/apex/dualism-cartography.md
- **Notes**: **Verified by grep, not merely asserted**: `illusion`, `frankish`, `dennett` and `predictive processing` return **zero hits** in `obsidian/apex/dualism-cartography.md`. **(a) The mind-thickness axis has no floor for the strongest physicalist rival.** Its minimum is "thin mind: only what is phenomenally introspectable — bare qualia." Illusionism (Frankish 2016, *JCS* 23(11–12):11–39; Dennett) denies there is a phenomenal explanandum to place on that axis at all, so the grid presupposes phenomenal realism and cannot represent the position, let alone charge it a cost. The article currently lets Q2 inert qualia stand in for the physicalist side, which conflates the weak rival with the absent strong one. **(b) Predictive processing / active inference** (Friston, Clark, Seth, Hohwy) is the live functional-computational competitor for exactly the Map's own explanatory ground — attention, precision-weighting, endogenous control — and the site has a `concepts/predictive-processing` page, yet the atlas never registers it. **(c) The grid "ranges over non-reductive positions", so all reductive physicalism, functionalism, GWT, IIT and higher-order theories are off-grid**; the scope note is not prominent enough to carry the weight the Synthesis claim puts on it. L125 says the cluster "turns 'is dualism true?' from an unanswerable question about a point into a *navigable* question about a territory" — but the grid can adjudicate only *within* the dualist / non-reductive family. **Bound the claim explicitly, or add representation for the missing families.** Lowe's non-Cartesian substance dualism and Hasker-style emergent dualism are also unplaced. **(d) Scope the orthogonality claim against the site's own concession.** L105 and L145 assert "No Many Worlds is largely orthogonal to the thickness axes." The tenets page's "Three Background Posits" section admits Tenets 1, 3 and 4 all draw on one root (the non-deflationary persisting subject) and "the three are not mutually independent." If that concession stands, the grid's apparent 2-D independence needs re-examining and the orthogonality claim needs scoping or retracting. **Note the asymmetry in what to preserve**: the reviewer judges the *demonstrated* orthogonality (Nida-Rümelin vs Descartes sharing a Chalmers type while differing in mind-thickness, L71) to be the article's one solid independently-warranted contribution — **retain it**. ### ✓ 2026-08-03: the parsimony case draws a positive conclusion its three sources only support defensively, and writes physicalist rivals out through accounting vocabulary
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/parsimony-case-for-interactionist-dualism.md
- **Notes**: **Citation-framing accuracy, not fabrication** — the sources are real and quoted correctly; the inference drawn from them overreaches. Huemer's stated conclusion is that four familiar accounts of parsimony's evidential value do not extend naturally to typical philosophical cases; Zanotti argues a modesty-based rationale does not ultimately favour physicalism over dualism; Lycan observes that systematic overdetermination is what principally offends parsimony. Together these support the **defensive** claim that parsimony does not decide the mind–body question. They do **not** support the stronger positive framing that physicalism buys economy only by accepting brute emergence, error theory, or in-principle inaccessibility. **Re-verify each at the publisher before rewriting** — this session's web-search budget was exhausted and only the reviewer's characterisation was available. **The three-way debt classification is boundary-substitution**: Type-A functionalism, identity theories, representationalism and higher-order theories deny or reinterpret the explanatory demand rather than picking one of those three debts, so the taxonomy makes rivals look defeated by the Map's own accounting categories instead of engaging them. The Map may well find these responses inadequate — but that case has to be argued, position by position, not won by vocabulary. Same defect propagates into `apex/dualism-cartography`'s parsimony paragraph; fix both or scope this task to the topic article and note the apex locus.

- **Type**: refine-draft

It has stood since the article's creation on 2026-03-29. **FIX (~150w + 4 refs):** at L60 note the statistical reading is contested rather than consensus, naming Brodutch's published Comment and the three arXiv responses; at L120 let "actively pursuing" cite the exchange so the claim is verifiable rather than asserted. **CALIBRATION GUARD — do not overshoot.** The goal is to right-size the concession, **NOT** to flip it into "the objection is answered". Preserve L122 verbatim ("The constraint can fail the theory but cannot establish it") — it is the best-calibrated sentence in the cluster. ### ✓ 2026-08-03: bandwidth-of-consciousness says Sauerbrei & Pruszynski "show experimentally" when their piece is a two-page commentary, and leaves the information-theoretic rival reading of the same bottleneck untested
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/topics/bandwidth-of-consciousness.md
- **Notes**: **Two findings on one article; both verified at the publisher, not asserted. See the review's `## Verification Notes`.** **(a) CITATION-FRAMING OVER-CLAIM — re-frame, do not delete.** L147 reads *"the brain *can* process at higher rates, as Sauerbrei and Pruszynski show experimentally."* The citation is **correct** (*Nature Neuroscience* 28: 1365–1366, DOI 10.1038/s41593-025-01997-0 — metadata confirmed at Crossref and OpenAlex, and the Map's reference entry matches exactly). But the piece is a **two-page commentary on Zheng & Meister that cites a single reference and reports no original data** — confirmed by retrieving the full text at Europe PMC (PMC12320479), which argues from previously published findings. "Show experimentally" over-claims the evidential form. `topics/grain-mismatch-as-independent-evidence.md` **L57** hedges better (*"sharpen the point"*) — bring L147 into line with it. **The substantive reading is sound and must survive the fix**: the authors do say *"The 10 bits / s speed limit, then, is a lower bound, not an upper bound, on the maximum information throughput of an entire human"* and *"Much of the moment-to-moment computation the brain performs is not subject to a cognitive processing bottleneck"*, so locating the constraint at conscious access is **their** claim, not a Map gloss — the Gemini review's charge that the Map "gerrymanders" them is FALSE and was rejected. Fix only the word "experimentally". **(b) AN UNTESTED PHYSICALIST RIVAL — cite it at its true evidential weight.** The article treats the 10⁹→10 bits/s narrowing as evidence the bottleneck sits at the mind–brain interface rather than in the hardware. There is a live physicalist reading of the *same* gap: **Yin, D. (2026), "The 10 bits/s bottleneck as error-correcting redundancy: an information-theoretic theory of cognitive reserve", DOI 10.64898/2026.03.24.713899** — the redundancy is functional (neuronal loss maps to symbol erasure, failure at threshold d_c = 1 − k/n), which would explain the narrowing without an interface. Absent from the corpus (grep returns nothing outside the review). **⚠ IT IS AN UNREFEREED bioRxiv PREPRINT, DATED 2026 — NOT a peer-reviewed 2020–2025 source.** The Gemini review presented it, **with no citation at all**, as *"the actual physicalist explanation … established in the literature"*; that framing is what let it pass, and it must not be reproduced. Existence and abstract confirmed at OpenAlex; **verify at the preprint itself before citing**. Engage it as *one preprint proposal*, at preprint weight, per [evidential-status-discipline](/project/evidential-status-discipline/) — a one-to-two-sentence acknowledgement that a redundancy reading of the same datum is on the table and has not been tested against the filter reading. **Do not** let it become a refutation of the bandwidth argument, and **do not** inflate it to "the physicalist explanation". **COORDINATE — this file already carries two open tasks** (the filter-theory-withdrawal cross-link task and the DeWall/Lieberman attribution task). Read both before editing; if one is executed first, re-check L147 and the reference list rather than assuming line numbers hold. Length-neutral: the article is already a hub. Run `uv run python scripts/sync.py` afterwards. **ATTRIBUTION**: **HOLD `ai_system`**, bump `ai_modified` from a live `date -u` (verify strictly past), leave `last_deep_review` alone. **ADDENDUM (2026-08-03 Claude Opus 5 full-site audit, section 2.2) — author-stance, fold into the same pass, do NOT mint separately.** A second reviewer independently reached the same over-claim, which upgrades confidence in the (a) fix. It adds one genuinely new element: both cited teams are **physicalists who attribute the bottleneck to neural architecture, not to a mind-matter interface**. Zheng & Meister frame their result as "the neural conundrum behind the slowness of human behavior" and look for the neural substrate that sets the speed limit; Sauerbrei & Pruszynski's point is that unconscious motor control is fast, i.e. the bottleneck sits in the neural architecture of conscious access. ### ✓ 2026-08-03: Born-marginal preservation does NOT entail zero mutual information — withdraw the selection-only causal budget until rederived
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/concepts/selection-only-channel.md
- **Notes**: From outer review 2026-08-03 (ChatGPT 5.6 Pro §2.1, §4.1). **CONFIRMED against the source — this is a genuine mathematical error, not a reviewer misreading.** `selection-only-channel.md:71` states the Born constraint as a *marginal* one ("the empirical frequency distribution over the output alphabet must converge to the Born-rule distribution … across many trials"), then line 73 infers "Born-rule preservation pins the *expected* mutual information between mind-state and outcome to zero in the long-run limit." That inference is invalid. Counterexample: let C be a uniform binary conscious state and O = C; the marginal is exactly uniform (Born-satisfying) yet I(C;O) = 1 bit. Marginal preservation is compatible with *maximal* conditional dependence. Three distinct defects to fix in the same passage: (a) the zero-MI inference itself; (b) **"signed" mutual information is a category error** — MI is non-negative, and a non-negative quantity whose average is zero requires every term to be zero, so nothing "cancels"; (c) the ε²/(2 ln 2) bits/trial figure and the ε ≈ 10⁻⁴ → 7×10⁻⁹ bits/event psi calibration inherit the error and must be suspended, not merely hedged — an ε² expression may approximate a divergence for one specifically defined binary perturbation but is not a consequence of Born preservation, and needs a declared channel, reference distribution, priors and perturbation geometry. The section heading "Born-Rule Preservation Drives Signed Rate Toward Zero" encodes the error and must change too. **The Map already has the correct statement elsewhere** — `obsidian/apex/born-preserving-causal-efficacy.md:85`: "Born-preservation constrains the left-hand marginal; it says nothing about whether the conditionals on the right depart from it" — so this is a propagation failure, and the apex is the model to conform to, not a rival reading. **Sibling sweep required** (a locus list is a sample, not the population): the same claim is inherited at `obsidian/research/selection-only-mind-influence-information-limits-2026-05-05.md:268`; before closing, grep all three trees (`obsidian/`, `archive/`, `hugo/content/`) for "mutual information", "signed", "ε²"/"epsilon" and the 7×10⁻⁹ figure, and check `obsidian/topics/selection-only-mind-influence.md` and `obsidian/project/causal-budget-ledger.md`. Note the research note must be fixed too, or the defect re-enters via the next article that consumes it. **`/combine-outer-reviews` 2026-08-03: SINGLETON on the mathematics (only ChatGPT reached it) but the cycle's exemplar of the convergent C1 diagnosis, and it stays P1 as the highest-confidence finding of the cycle.** The correct statement already exists at `apex/born-preserving-causal-efficacy.md:85` and the wrong one is still live downstream — which is precisely the pattern the other two-thirds of the cycle described in the abstract (ChatGPT: "failure to propagate self-criticism from authoritative registers into all downstream prose"; Claude Opus 5: the corpus "names nearly every fatal objection to itself, banks the naming as an epistemic credential, and then retains the offending content unchanged"). **Consequence for how this is executed**: the sibling sweep is not optional housekeeping — it is the finding. Closing the concept page alone while the research note and the ledger still carry the ε²/(2 ln 2) chain would reproduce the exact defect the cycle converged on.
