Grading a three-week-old industry technical post at that tier flattens the axis the 2026-07-16 migration was created to sharpen. **Two corroborating asymmetries**: the corpus **does** apply venue caveats elsewhere — `topics/ai-consciousness.md` **L115** and **L249** flag Hoel 2025 twice as *"a preprint not yet peer-reviewed"* — yet **no venue caveat attaches to Gurnee anywhere in `obsidian/`**; and [P-M3](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m3) mandates the missing discipline in terms (*"a convergence node resting on a single-case or small-N source must acknowledge that fragility explicitly"*), so the register is failing to apply its own weight-class rule to its most load-bearing external-evidence claim. **FIX**: downgrade to **Grade B** ("some independent support (realistic possibility)") and add a one-clause venue flag to the calibration note — un-refereed vendor publication, one independent replication (Nanda), ~1 month old at grading. Grade A becomes available on refereeing or independent replication by a second group. The reference entry itself (`concepts/access-consciousness.md` L157) already names the venue honestly and needs no change. **(B) THE HEADING ASSERTS A GRADE-D CLAUSE CATEGORICALLY.** **L86** reads *"[P-AC4](/positions/ai-consciousness-scope/#p-ac4): Current LLMs exhibit the workspace-like functional signatures of access consciousness — **and that leaves the phenomenal question untouched**."* The body is careful that this second clause is the framework-conditional half (*"the phenomenal residue is real and separate, not illusory — is the framework-conditional part, held at Tenet 1's confidence"*, Grade D), but the heading states it flatly, and it presupposes a phenomenal question distinct from access — exactly what illusionism denies and what the entry's own *Would shift if* names as a defeater. Position headings are a navigation surface and the string apex articles quote. **Re-label, do not re-scope**: e.g. *"— and on the Map's reading that leaves the phenomenal question open"*. The `description:` frontmatter at **L3** already gets this right ("while the phenomenal question stays open"); make the heading match it.

### ✓ 2026-08-03: the calibration schema is violated in its own defining file — six off-vocabulary band values across four registers, and [P-M3](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m3)/[P-M5](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m5) each state their confidence three inconsistent ways
- **Type**: positions-evolve
- **File**: obsidian/positions/methodology-and-calibration.md
- **Notes**: All values **measured on disk 2026-08-03**, not inferred. The register-wide sweep is in scope (positions-evolve's audit remit); `File` names the defining file because that is where the schema and the worst instances live. **(A) SIX OFF-VOCABULARY BAND VALUES.** The template at `positions.md` **L39** closes the vocabularies: `model maturity <formalised/developed/programme>` and `empirical discriminability <direct/indirect/in-principle/none-by-construction/n a>`. Violations: **`model maturity moderate` ×2** — `methodology-and-calibration.md` **L77 ([P-M3](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m3))** and **L97 ([P-M5](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m5))**, i.e. four lines below the definition that excludes the value; **`empirical discriminability none` ×4** (schema has only `none-by-construction`) — `individuation-and-subjecthood.md` **L40**, `agency-and-will.md` **L60 ([P-A2](/positions/agency-and-will/#p-a2))**, `ai-consciousness-scope.md` **L79 ([P-AC3](/positions/ai-consciousness-scope/#p-ac3))**, `consciousness-scope.md` **L48**. **Do not reflexively force-fit the four `none` cases** — they look like a genuine missing distinction: *no test possible in principle* ([P-A2](/positions/agency-and-will/#p-a2)'s metaphysical requirement claim) is not the same as *test excluded by design* (Born-preservation's `none-by-construction`). The better fix is probably to **add a `none` band** to the schema at `methodology-and-calibration.md` L44 and `positions.md` L39 and leave the four entries alone; decide explicitly and record the decision. **(B) [P-M3](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m3) AND [P-M5](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m5) EACH STATE CONFIDENCE THREE WAYS.** [P-M3](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m3) (L77–81): calibration line says `credence high (the standard is right)`; Asserts L78 says *"**Confidence is moderate, not high**"*; Would-shift-if L81 says *"would **raise this toward high**"*. [P-M5](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m5) (L97–101): calibration line `credence high (the principle is firmly held)`; Asserts L98 *"**Confidence is moderate**"*; Would-shift-if L101 *"**raise this toward high**"*. Root cause is the same as (A): the 2026-07-16 migration decomposed the old single band into `credence high` + a maturity qualifier and never swept the Asserts prose or the shift conditions, which are still keyed to the retired band. **Bounded** — `grep "Confidence is \*low/moderate/high\*" obsidian/positions/` hits this file only. **The defensible reading** is `credence high` (the standard/principle genuinely is right — that is what credence measures) with the enforcement gap carried on its own axis and the shift conditions re-keyed to it. **(C) NOTE WHERE THE DEMOTING JUDGEMENT WENT.** The enforcement gap — the honest content of both entries — was parked in `model maturity moderate` precisely because **the schema has no axis for enforcement or adoption status**. The two positions whose entire subject is the disclosure/enforcement gap were the two forced off-vocabulary to express it. Consider adding a seventh flag (`enforcement: wired / partial / unwired`) rather than overloading model maturity; [P-M5](/positions/methodology-and-calibration/#p-m5) would then declare `enforcement: partial` about the register's own schema, which is the honest result. **(D) AMEND THE COMPLETENESS CLAIM.** `positions.md` **L49** states *"the migration is complete."* True of the fields, false of the write path, the auditor and the validator (see the NEEDS-HUMAN above). Qualify to "the field migration is complete; the write path and validator are not yet migrated." **(E) SYNC BOTH TREES.** `methodology-and-calibration.md`'s two bad values are mirrored live at `hugo/content/positions/methodology-and-calibration.md` **L81, L101** — an obsidian-only fix leaves the defect served until the next pre-push sync; run the sync in the same pass.

### ✓ 2026-08-03: dualism-cartography converts Lycan from hostile materialist witness into a dualist defender, and presents the contested Born-rule uniqueness result as settled
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/apex/dualism-cartography.md
- **Notes**: Two confirmed citation defects, **both web-verified at the publisher of record during this review's processing** — cheap, concrete, and distinct from the citation gaps already queued from the same-cycle ChatGPT review (which cover Eccles, Stapp's reference-11 metadata, the missing theta-band citation, and the conservation reply). **(a) Lycan stance-misframe — citation-framing accuracy, not fabrication.** L99 reads "parsimony's own advocates have qualified the argument—**Lycan, defending Cartesian interactive dualism**, concedes that 'there is no evidence against that view; it only offends parsimony.'" The **quotation is verbatim and may stand** — a 2026-W23 pass already corrected its wording from "against dualism" to "against that view" — but the framing inverts the author. Verified in the full text of "Giving Dualism its Due" (AJP 87(4):551–563): Lycan writes "I have been a materialist about the mind for forty years", "My materialism has never wavered", describes the paper as "an uncharacteristic exercise in intellectual honesty", and closes "I have no sympathy with any dualist view, and never will." Replace with an accurate framing — Lycan as a committed materialist conceding a point against his own side. **This makes the citation stronger, not weaker**: a hostile witness's concession carries more weight than an advocate's. The defect is live in `hugo/content/apex/dualism-cartography.md:103` too, so **sync after fixing** rather than leaving the published page carrying it. Re-grep the string `defending Cartesian` across `obsidian/`, `archive/` and `hugo/content/` before closing — a review's locus list is a sample, not the population (this sweep found two loci, both the same sentence). **(b) Masanes–Galley–Müller presented as settled when it is under live published challenge.** L119 calls the Born rule "the *unique* consistent probability rule given basic quantum postulates", and the Evidence-and-Dependency ledger at L133 banks "Born-rule uniqueness" among the article's externally-evidenced constraints. Reference 7 is Masanes, Galley & Müller (2019), *Nature Communications* 10:1361, "The measurement postulates of quantum mechanics are operationally **redundant**". Two published rebuttals verified this session: **Adrian Kent, arXiv:2307.06191 (2023), "The measurement postulates of quantum mechanics are not redundant"**, which supplies "explicit examples of non-quantum measurement and state update rules that satisfy all their assumptions"; and **Blake C. Stacey, arXiv:2211.03299 (2022, rev. 2023), "Masanes-Galley-Müller and the State-Update Postulate"**, whose abstract states the proof "implicitly assumes its first step, namely that the state-update rule is linear." MGM have replied (arXiv:2309.01650, arXiv:2212.03629; further reply *Quantum* 9:1592, 2025), so this is a live dispute, not a refutation — **do not flip the article to saying MGM is wrong.** Downgrade "unique" to language registering the open contest, and note the result's finite-dimensional-ensemble assumption. The frontier's Born-rule ceiling argument rests on this claim and currently overstates it; adjust the ledger entry at L133 to match.

### ✓ 2026-08-03: dualism-cartography's "mechanism cost" is a metric in name only, and the battery it is scored with never charges the Map's own cell
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/apex/dualism-cartography.md
- **Notes**: The review's headline finding (verdict REVISE-HARD, cost overlay DEMOTE-TO-COHERENCE-ONLY). All quoted loci grep-verified — see the review's `## Verification Notes`. **(a) Define the metric or drop the comparative vocabulary.** The Synthesis promises the overlay makes "costs become comparable rather than rhetorical" (L125), but the article supplies no unit, no scale (ordinal or cardinal), no zero point, no aggregation rule across the three battery questions, and no comparability condition. The costs are verbal tags — authority debt, exclusion debt, appearance debt, "the full bill" — and the comparative claims resting on them ("Q1 is cheaper than Q4" L101, "the cheapest permitted cell" L97, Q4 "owes the heaviest version of all three debts") have no scale on which "cheaper" or "heaviest" is defined. Either state the comparability condition and the aggregation rule, or rename the overlay a **debt inventory** and delete every comparative-magnitude claim. Note the per-cell asymmetry the reviewer concedes: Q1's authority debt (via Schaffer's trumping preemption) and Q2's exclusion debt (via Kim) **are** genuinely argued; Q3's appearance/transparency debt and Q4's "full bill" are largely asserted. Fixing the vocabulary should not flatten that difference — say which cells' costs are argued and which are stipulated. **(b) Calibration parity — the battery contains no line item that charges the Map's own cell.** L83 concedes the trio "leans deliberately toward physical-side debts" and then defends parity with the bare assertion "but on inspection it appears to track a real difference." That sentence is the entire parity argument. Two of the Map's heaviest liabilities are costed nowhere on the grid: (i) the **Born-rule statistical-indistinguishability dilemma** — consciousness must bias post-decoherence outcomes while producing zero aggregate deviation from Born statistics, which the tenets page and the post-decoherence apex both concede in stark terms, and which is a first-order cost of the Q1 cell the Map inhabits; (ii) **rejecting Many-Worlds (Tenet 4)**, which `topics/four-quadrant-dualism-taxonomy` already admits rests on "the global-exclusion posit… a chosen starting point, not a result this grid delivers." Add both as battery line items, or state plainly that the battery scores rivals only and withdraw the parity claim. **A battery that never charges the author's cell is not measuring cost; it is measuring rivals.** **(c) The disclaimer and the ranking cannot both stand as written.** The article disclaims adjudication ("A Map, Not a Verdict", L123) while the parsimony chapter concludes the Map pays "the *least mechanism cost compatible with its tenets*" (L101) and the overlay names Q1 the cheapest permitted cell — with the Map located at Q1. Either the ranking is doing evidential work, in which case the disclaimer needs qualifying, or it is not, in which case the least-cost conclusion should be marked as internal bookkeeping rather than a result. Do **not** resolve this by deleting the self-critical passages: the honest move is to convert the existing confessions into a binding change to the cost assignments, which is exactly what the review says the article currently fails to do ("confession-without-correction"). **SCOPE:** length-neutral where possible; the apex is a flagship, check `analyze_length` before adding. Two other open tasks target this file (the ChatGPT P1 on the Saad/quantum fusion and the P2 on the citation apparatus) — **if a pass is already open on this article, fold this in rather than editing it four times.**

### ✓ 2026-08-03: interface-formalization-void misattributes the Entropy category-theory paper and overstates "convergent failure" of formalisation
- **Type**: refine-draft
- **File**: obsidian/voids/interface-formalization-void.md
- **Notes**: **(a) CONFIRMED citation defect — cheap and concrete.** L77 and reference 7 credit "Chang, A.Y.C. & Tsuchiya, N. (2019), *Entropy* 21(12), 1234" for "Mathematics and the Brain: A Category Theoretical Approach." Verified at OpenAlex (`doi:10.3390/e21121234`): same title, journal, volume, issue, article number and year, but the authors are **Georg Northoff, Naotsugu Tsuchiya, Hayato Saigo**. Venue metadata is right; the author list is wrong. Fix both loci, and re-grep the wrong author string across `obsidian/`, `archive/` and `hugo/content/` before closing — a locus list from a review is a sample, not the population. **(b) The "convergent failure of every formal framework tried so far" claim is stronger than the cited record.** That phrase also propagates into `apex/dualism-cartography`. Kleiner's framework work and Kleiner & Ludwig (2023) present formalisation as extensible, and Chalmers & McQueen report that while *simple* IIT–CSL models are falsified by the quantum Zeno effect, more complex variants survive and are experimentally testable — a live programme, not a uniform failure. Replace "convergent failure" with an accurate account of incomplete-but-active programmes. **(c) Separate two claims the article currently slides between**: that mathematics may not exhaust the intrinsic phenomenal character of experience, and that mathematics *cannot* formulate a law relating phenomenal to physical variables. The first does not entail the second — a formal theory can take phenomenal states as primitive relata and still specify mappings and probabilities. The article itself says its non-structural premise is supplied by the tenets, so treating the failure of structural mathematics to deliver stipulated non-structural actuality as *independent* evidence of a ceiling is circular. Keep the void; narrow its warrant to what it has earned.
